, the sieve model class in the likelihood-based approach admits a natural deconvolution interpretation when ambient noise is present, while diffusion models target the observed distribution. Consequently, diffusion models perform extremely well in estimating the intrinsic distribution in the case of no noise or small noise levels, but struggle to recover the intrinsic distribution for moderate to high noise levels. On the other hand, VAEs provide better estimation of the intrinsic distribution as long as the noise level is not too small.

To illustrate these behaviors, we consider the 1-dimensional helix and 2-dimensional Swiss roll from Figure 4, but embedded in
\mathbb{R}^{15}
with mixture weights
\omega_{1}=\omega_{2}=0.5
and Leaky ReLU networks with
a=0.2
with 4 hidden layers of width 512. We run standard DDPM, while the mixture of VAEs was trained with
\beta=0.01
for the first 2000 epochs, then 2000 more with frozen routing while annealing
\beta
to 0.5. The (sliced) Wasserstein-1 distance over various noise levels is portrayed in Figure 5 at
n=1000
and
n=3000
samples. We see that the mixture of VAEs attains small error at both of these sample sizes for noise levels up to around
\sigma_{*}=0.2
, but the gap is most pronounced at
\sigma=0.5
, where
W_{1}
drops substantially as
n
increases from
n=1000
to
n=3000
. Also, the diffusion model is more accurate at low noise but eventually becomes worse than VAE as
\sigma_{*}
grows. The curves cross around
\sigma_{*}=0.15
when
n=3000
, and at even small
\sigma_{*}
when
n=1000
, after which the diffusion model’s sliced Wasserstein-1 distance increases steadily. This behavior is consistent with the fact that diffusion model learn the smoothed, noised-corrupted distribution rather than the underlying intrinsic one. Furthermore, while the VAE’s error naturally grows at extreme noise levels (
\sigma_{*}=0.5
), this gap shrinks significantly as the sample size increases.

Figure 5
:
Comparison of the (Sliced) Wasserstein-1 distance between learned distribution and the intrinsic distribution via diffusion models and Mixture of VAEs at
n=1000
and
n=3000
samples.

7
Discussion

This paper develops a generative framework for
stratified learning
. Going beyond the classical manifold setting, we study distributions supported on or near unions of manifolds of different dimensions, possibly with singular intersections. Our results show that deep generative models can adapt not only to low-dimensional geometry, but also to more general stratified structures. In particular, the paper provides two generative approaches: a likelihood-based framework through a sieve MLE and a diffusion-based framework through score estimation. Together, these approaches elucidate how geometry, singularity, and ambient noise interact in determining the statistical theory of stratified learning.

A main message of the paper is that the two frameworks are suited to different regimes. The sieve-MLE approach provides a natural likelihood-based model for the ambient distribution and yields a direct estimator of the intrinsic distribution through the learned generator. This makes it particularly appealing when the data are generated with non-negligible Gaussian noise (when the noise delays to zero at a moderate rate) and one wishes to interpret the problem as deconvolution. At the same time, the analysis also reveals the inherent disadvantages of likelihood-based methods in the near-singular regime: when the noise level is too small, the ambient density becomes sharply concentrated near the stratified support, which makes maximum-likelihood estimation unstable. In contrast, the diffusion-based framework remains well posed even in the noiseless setting, since the forward process itself regularizes the distribution through Gaussian smoothing. This makes diffusion models especially attractive for learning distributions on highly singular or nearly singular stratified spaces.

Another important insight from our analysis is that ambient noise does not play a purely detrimental role. In the likelihood framework, too much noise obscures the underlying geometry, while too little noise creates instability through singularity. For near-singular case, the injected noise in a data perturbation approach becomes a blessing. In the diffusion framework, moderate noise can have a favorable effect by smoothing the target distribution and stabilizing score estimation. In particular, when the noise level is of constant order, the Wasserstein bound attains the parametric root-
n
rate up to logarithmic factors for the ambient distribution estimation. Overall, these results highlight a subtle geometry-noise interaction: ambient noise may either regularize or obscure the problem depending on the learning framework and the noise regime under consideration.

Beyond distribution estimation, our work also shows that diffusion models provide a principled route to learning the geometric information of the structures. By exploiting the local behavior of the score field at small diffusion times, we establish consistency for estimating local intrinsic dimension and, consistency for estimating the number of strata. This connects generative modeling with geometric structure learning in a way that is not done by classical manifold-learning approaches. At the same time, our analysis highlights that dimension estimation is most reliable at regular points, while points near singular intersections remain intrinsically more delicate due to the competition between nearby strata.

There are several interesting directions for future research. These include extending the analysis to stratified spaces under more general geometric assumptions, as well as developing more flexible noise models that allow for varying and anisotropic noise across strata. It would also be of interest to study more general mixture distributions that permit small mixing weights, and to characterize the corresponding minimax rates under suitable beta-min conditions on the mixing weights and separation conditions on the mixture components. Beyond the development of statistical theory, it is also important to investigate the computational and optimization aspects of the proposed models.

Appendix A
Proofs for Section 3

A.1
Proof of Lemma
4

Suppose
\|f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq V
and takes the form

f^{*}(w,z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}h^{*}_{k,j}(w)f^{*}_{k,j}(z).

For each
k\in\{1,...,K\}
and
j=1,...,J_{k}
, Lemma 5 in
12
implies there exists a neural network
f_{k,j}\in\mathcal{F}^{(k)}=\Phi(L^{(k)},W^{(k)},R^{(k)},1,V^{(k)})
with
L^{(k)}=O(\log\delta^{-1})
,
\|W^{(k)}\|_{\infty}=O(\delta^{-d_{k}/(\alpha_{k}+1)})
,
R^{(k)}=O(\delta^{-d_{k}/(\alpha_{k}+1)}\log\delta^{-1})
satisfying
\|f_{k,j}-f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\delta}{2KJ_{k}}
.

Although one can approximate
\mathbf{1}_{[a,b)}
with a shallow ReLU network with bounded parameters, it would require a large width and sparsity to create a steep slope, which dominates order of the overall error. However, we can use depth in order to stay within bounded weights:

Lemma 19
.

Fix
0\leq a<b\leq 1
,
1\leq p<\infty
and
0<\xi<1
. Then there exists
h\in\Phi(L,W,R,1,1)
with
L=O(\log\xi^{-1})
,
\|W\|_{\infty}=O(1)
and
R=O(\log\xi^{-1})
such that

1.

h(x)=1
for
x\in[a,b]

2.

h(x)=0
for
x\leq a-\xi
and
x\geq b+\xi
.

3.

\|h-\mathbf{1}_{[a,b]}\|_{L^{p}}\leq(2\xi)^{1/p}

From Lemma
19
with
\xi\asymp\delta^{2}
, for each
k=1,...,K
and
j=1,...,J_{k}
, there exists
h_{k,j}
with
\widetilde{L}=O(\log\delta^{-1})
,
\|\widetilde{W}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
and
\widetilde{R}=O(\log\delta^{-1})
such that

\|h_{k,j}-h^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\delta}{2VKJ_{k}}.

Let
f(w,z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}h_{k,j}(w)f_{k,j}(z)
. Note that
h^{*}_{k,j}
have mutually disjoint support for each
k,j
, and so
\|f_{k,j}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq V
. It then follows that

\displaystyle\|f-f^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\|h_{k,j}f_{k,j}-h^{*}_{k,j}f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\|h_{k,j}f_{k,j}-h_{k,j}f^{*}_{k,j}+h_{k,j}f^{*}_{k,j}-h^{*}_{k,j}f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\left(\|h_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\|f_{k,j}-f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}+\|f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\|h_{k,j}-h^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\left(1\cdot\|f_{k,j}-f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}+\|f^{*}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\|h_{k,j}-h^{*}_{k,j}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\left(\frac{\delta}{2KJ_{k}}+V\frac{\delta}{2VKJ_{k}}\right)=\delta.

\hfill{\Box}

A.2
Proof of Lemma
6

Recall the following Lemma from Chae et. al:

Lemma 20
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of functions from
\mathcal{Z}
to
\mathbb{R}^{D}
such that
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq V
for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Let
\mathcal{P}=\{P_{f,\sigma}:f\in\mathcal{F},\sigma\in[\sigma_{min},\sigma_{max}]\}
with
\sigma_{min}\leq 1
. There, there exists constants
c=c(D,V,\sigma_{max})
,
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime}(D,V,\sigma_{max})
and
\delta_{*}=\delta_{*}(D)
such that

\log N_{[]}(\delta,\mathcal{P},d_{H})\leq\log N(c\sigma_{min}^{D+3}\delta^{4},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})+\log\Big(\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sigma_{min}^{D+2}\delta^{4}}\Big)

for every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{*}].

Note that this lemma holds for any class of bounded functions
\mathcal{F}
from
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}^{D}
. Given a stratified space consisting of
K
manifolds and a decomposition of each manifold into
J_{k}
charts, a function
f
in the mixture-of-experts sieve
\mathcal{G}^{K,\mathbf{J}}
is of the form
f(w,z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}h_{k,j}(w)f_{k,j}(z)
, where
h_{k,j}\in\mathcal{H}
and
f_{k,j}\in\mathcal{F}^{(k)}
for each
k=1,...,K
and
j=1,...,J_{k}
. Since
\|h_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|f_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\leq V
, for any
\tilde{f}(w,z)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\tilde{h}_{k,j}(w)\tilde{f}_{k,j}(z)\in\mathcal{G}^{K,\mathbf{J}}
,

\displaystyle\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}(h_{k,j}f_{k,j}-\tilde{h}_{k,j}\tilde{f}_{k,j})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\|h_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\|f_{k,j}-\tilde{f}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}+\|\tilde{f}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}\|h_{k,j}-\tilde{h}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\|f_{k,j}-\tilde{f}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}+V\|h_{k,j}-\tilde{h}_{k,j}\|_{\infty}

Let
M=\sum_{k=1}^{K}J_{k}
. Taking an
\frac{\epsilon}{2M}
-cover of
\mathcal{F}^{(k)}
and
\frac{\epsilon}{2VM}
-cover of
\mathcal{H}
for each
k=1,...,K
and
j=1,...,J_{k}
gives us an
\epsilon
-cover for
\mathcal{G}^{K,\mathbf{J}}
, and hence

\displaystyle\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}^{K,\mathbf{J}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\log N\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2M},\mathcal{F}^{(k)},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)+M\log N\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2VM},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}J_{k}\log N\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2M},\mathcal{F}^{(k)},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)+M\log N\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2VM},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)

Applying Lemma
20
and setting
\epsilon=c\sigma_{\min}^{D+3}\delta^{4}
gives the claim after absorbing constants.

\hfill{\Box}

A.3
Proof of Theorem
7

(1) Follows from Lemma
6
and (2) follows from Lemma
4
with
\delta=\delta_{\operatorname{app}}
.

For (3), we follow the idea in the proofs of Theorem 3 and Corollary 6 in
12
and provide their proofs for completeness. To this end, choose constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}
with
c_{1}=1/3
and
c_{3}>2
as in Theorem 1 in
54
and constants
c,c^{\prime}
in their Lemma 1.

Let
\hat{R}=\widetilde{R}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}J_{k}R^{(k)}
be the total sparsity. For
\delta\in(0,c_{3}\delta_{*}]
,

\log N_{[]}\left(\frac{\delta}{c_{3}},\mathcal{P},d_{H}\right)\leq 4(\hat{R}+1)\log\delta^{-1}+\hat{R}A+(D+3)(\hat{R}+1)\log\sigma_{\min}^{-1}+c_{5}\hat{R}

for some
c_{5}=c_{5}(c,c^{\prime},c_{3})
by (1). Thus, for all
\epsilon\leq\sqrt{2}\delta_{*}\leq c_{3}\delta_{*}/\sqrt{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\epsilon^{2}/2^{8}}^{\sqrt{2}\epsilon}\sqrt{\log N_{[]}(\delta/c_{3},\mathcal{P},d_{H})}\,d\delta

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon\sqrt{\hat{R}A+(D+3)(\hat{R}+1)\log\sigma_{\min}^{-1}+c_{5}\hat{R}}+\sqrt{2}\epsilon\sqrt{4(\hat{R}+1)}\sqrt{\log\frac{2^{8}}{\epsilon^{2}}}

This can be bounded by
c_{4}n^{1/2}\epsilon_{n}^{2}
, where we take
\epsilon=\epsilon_{n}=c_{6}\sqrt{n^{-1}\hat{R}(A+\log(n/\sigma_{\min}))}
, for a large enough constant
c_{6}=c_{6}(c_{4},c_{5},D)
. Then by Example B.12 in
17
,

\displaystyle\text{KL}(p_{*},p_{f,\sigma_{*}})

\displaystyle\leq\int\text{KL}(N(f^{*}(z),\sigma_{*}^{2}),N(f(z),\sigma_{*}^{2}))dP_{Z}(z)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\|f^{*}(z)-f(z)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}dP_{Z}(z)\leq\frac{\delta_{\text{app}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}.

We also have that

\int\left(\log\frac{\phi_{\sigma}(x)}{\phi_{\sigma}(x-y)}\right)^{2}\phi_{\sigma}(x)dx=\int\frac{\|y\|_{2}^{4}+4|x^{T}y|^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\phi_{\sigma}(x)dx\leq\frac{\|y\|^{4}_{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}+\|y\|_{2}^{2}\int\frac{\|x\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\phi_{\sigma}(x)dx.

Thus, by Example B.12, (B.17) and Exercise B.8 in
17
,

\displaystyle\int\left(\log\frac{p_{*}(x)}{p_{f,\sigma_{*}}(x)}\right)^{2}dP_{*}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\iint\left(\log\frac{\phi_{\sigma_{*}}(x-f_{*}(z)}{\phi_{\sigma_{*}}(x-f(z))}\right)^{2}\phi_{\sigma}(x-f_{*}(z))dxdP_{z}+4\text{KL}(p_{*},p_{f,\sigma_{*}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{\text{app}}^{4}}{4\sigma_{*}^{2}}+\delta_{\text{app}}^{2}\int\frac{\|x\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}\phi_{\sigma_{*}}(x)dx+\frac{2\delta_{\text{app}}^{2}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}\leq c_{7}\frac{\delta_{\text{app}}^{2}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}},

for some constant
c_{7}
. Let
\delta_{n}=\frac{\delta_{\text{app}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}
and
\tau_{n}=c_{7}\frac{\delta^{2}_{\text{app}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}
. Then by Theorem 4 in
54
with
\epsilon_{n}^{*}=\epsilon_{n}\vee\sqrt{12\delta_{n}}
,

P_{*}(d_{H}(\hat{p},p_{*})>\epsilon_{n}^{*})\leq 5e^{-c_{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{*2}}+\frac{12\tau_{n}}{n\epsilon_{n}^{*2}}\leq 5e^{-c_{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{*2}}+\frac{\tau_{n}}{n\delta_{n}}=5e^{-c_{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{*2}}+\frac{2c_{7}}{n}.

The Theorem follows after redefining constants.
\hfill{\Box}

A.4
Proof of Theorem
8

We will make use of the following lemma to separate the contributions from the partitioned space:

Lemma 21
.

Let
Y\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{D}
be a compact set, let
R=\text{diam}(Y)
, and let
\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}Q_{k}
be a mixture distribution on
Y
. Then for any mixture distribution
\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k}
that is also supported on
Y
,

W_{1}(\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}Q_{k})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}W_{1}(\tilde{Q}_{k},Q_{k})+\frac{R}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|a_{k}-b_{k}|.

We proceed to assume that
\epsilon
and
\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
are sufficiently small; otherwise we just need a suitably large enough constant
C
so that the inequality will hold. Recall from the proof of Theorem 7 in
12
that for the choice
t_{*}=(2D\sigma_{*}^{2}\log(D/\epsilon))^{1/2}
, we have that

\int_{\|x\|_{2}\geq t_{*}}\phi_{\sigma_{*}}(x)dx\leq\epsilon.

Throughout the proof, we fix the following parameters:

\eta_{1}=2t_{*},\quad\eta_{0}=4t_{*},\quad\rho=8t_{*},

and assume that
\rho<\min\{r_{*},1,\rho_{1}\}
. Suppose
\sigma\leq C_{0}\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log(D/\epsilon)}
for some constant
C_{0}
. This implies for
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{D}),Z_{*}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{*}^{2}I_{D})
that there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})\geq c_{0},\quad\mathbb{P}(\|Z_{*}\|\geq t_{*})\leq\epsilon.

To handle intersections, we define for each manifold
M_{k}
:

A_{k}=\left\{x\in M_{k}\mid\text{dist}\left(x,\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell}\right)\geq 2\rho\right\},\quad A=\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}A_{k},\quad I_{\mathrm{sing}}=\mathcal{S}\setminus A.

Remark 22
.

If two manifolds are sufficiently close without intersection, this excision would remove part of the manifold away from the intersections. However, we will assume enough of a margin and let
\rho
be sufficiently small so that this excision only occurs at around singularities.

Recall that we assume each stratum is compact with regularity assumptions along with intersections being transversal in Assumptions 1 and 2. This implies the local metric inequality
\text{dist}(x,M_{k}\cap(\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell}))\lesssim\text{dist}(x,\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell})
(see Theorem 1 in
(
30
)
). By compactness, this local bound holds globally. We also have the reverse bound induced by the inclusion
M_{k}\cap(\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell})\subset\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell}
, which implies
\text{dist}(x,M_{k}\cap(\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell}))\asymp\text{dist}(x,\bigcup_{\ell\neq k}M_{\ell})
. Hence,
A_{k}
is (up to a constant depending on the angle
\theta_{0}
between strata at intersections) the complement of a
2\rho
-tubular neighborhood of the singularity
\mathcal{S}_{\text{sing}}
in
M_{k}
. Combining this with
\text{reach}(M_{k})=r_{k}>\rho
and standard properties of sets with positive reach
(
16
)
, we have that
\text{reach}(A_{k})\geq\rho
. Since the sets
A_{k}
are separated by
\rho
, we conclude that

\text{reach}(A)\geq\min\left\{\min_{k}\text{reach}(A_{k}),\rho\right\}\geq\rho.

Since
f(\mathcal{Z})
need not lie entirely in
\mathcal{S}
, we partition
\mathbb{R}^{D}
as follows. Let

U=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}:\operatorname{dist}(x,A)<\eta_{0}\},\quad U^{c}=\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus U.

Also, let

a_{1}=Q_{f}(U),\quad a_{2}=Q_{f}(U^{c}),\quad b_{1}=Q_{*}(A),\quad b_{2}=Q_{*}(I_{\mathrm{sing}}),

and the normalized restrictions

Q_{f}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{a_{1}}Q_{f}|_{U},\quad Q_{f}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{a_{2}}Q_{f}|_{U^{c}},\quad Q_{*}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{b_{1}}Q_{*}|_{A},\quad Q_{*}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{b_{2}}Q_{*}|_{I_{\mathrm{sing}}}.

This implies that
a_{1}+a_{2}=Q_{f}(\mathbb{R}^{D})=1
and
b_{1}+b_{2}=Q_{*}(\mathcal{S})=1
. Since
\|f\|_{\infty},\|f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq V
, all measures are supported in
Y=\overline{B(0,V)}
. So by Lemma
21
with
R=\operatorname{diam}(Y)\leq 2V
, we have that

W_{1}\left(Q_{f},Q_{*}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}b_{i}W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(i)},Q_{*}^{(i)})+\frac{R}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|a_{i}-b_{i}|.

Bounding
b_{2}
and
b_{2}W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(2)},Q_{*}^{(2)})
.

Recall that the densities on each manifold are bounded from above and that intersections have positive codimension. Since the set
I_{\mathrm{sing}}
is essentially a union of geodesic tubes of radius
\rho
around lower-dimensional subsets, its volume within
M_{k}
is
O(\rho^{d})
for some
d\geq 1
. Thus, there exists a constant
c
such that

b_{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\int_{I_{\mathrm{sing}}\cap M_{k}}q_{k}\,d\text{vol}_{M_{k}}(x)\leq c\sum_{k=1}^{K}\text{vol}_{M_{k}}(I_{\mathrm{sing}}\cap M_{k})\lesssim\rho

As
Q_{f}^{(2)}
and
Q_{*}^{(2)}
are supported in
Y
, we have
W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(2)},Q_{*}^{(2)})\leq 2V
, and so

b_{2}W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(2)},Q_{*}^{(2)})\leq 2Vb_{2}\lesssim 2V\cdot\rho\lesssim\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}.

Bounding
a_{2}
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}:\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})<\eta_{1}\}
and consider the splitting

a_{2}=\underbrace{Q_{f}(U^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}})}_{\tilde{a}_{2}}+\underbrace{Q_{f}(\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}})}_{\hat{a}_{2}},

where
\tilde{a}_{2}
is the proportion of points closer to
\mathcal{S}
, and
\hat{a}_{2}
is the proportion farther away.

If
X\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus S^{\eta_{1}}
and
\|Z\|<t_{*}
, we have
\operatorname{dist}(X+Z,\mathcal{S})\geq\operatorname{dist}(X,\mathcal{S})-\|Z\|>t_{*}=\eta_{1}/2
, so
X+Z\not\in\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}/2}
. Hence

P_{f,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}/2})\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}}}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})Q_{f}(dx)=\hat{a}_{2}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*}).

Conversely, since
Q_{*}
is supported on
\mathcal{S}
,

P_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}/2})=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}(X+Z_{*}\notin\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}/2})Q_{*}(dx)\leq\mathbb{P}(\|Z_{*}\|\geq t_{*})\leq\epsilon.

With our bound on Hellinger distance, we have that
|P_{f,\sigma}(\cdot)-P_{*}(\cdot)|\leq d_{TV}(P_{f,\sigma},P_{*})\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon
, which implies that

\hat{a}_{2}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})\leq\epsilon+\sqrt{2}\epsilon.

As
\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})\geq c_{0}>0
, we have

\hat{a}_{2}\lesssim\epsilon.

Now for the mass near
\mathcal{S}
, we define the set

V=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}:\operatorname{dist}(x,I_{\mathrm{sing}})<3t_{*}\}.

Consider
y\in U^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}}
and let
x\in\mathcal{S}
be such that
\|x-y\|<\eta_{1}
. If
x\in A
, then
\operatorname{dist}(x,A)\leq\|x-y\|<\eta_{1}<\eta_{0}
, so
y\not\in U^{c}
, a contradiction. Hence
x\in I_{\mathrm{sing}}
and
\operatorname{dist}(y,I_{\mathrm{sing}})<2t_{*}
, so that
y\in V
. For any
X\in U^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}}
and any
Z
, we hence have

\|Z\|<t_{*}\implies\operatorname{dist}(X+Z,I_{\mathrm{sing}})\leq\operatorname{dist}(X,I_{\mathrm{sing}})+\|Z\|<3t_{*},

which implies that
X+Z\in V
. Therefore,

P_{f,\sigma}(V)\geq\int_{U^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\eta_{1}}}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})Q_{f}(dx)=\tilde{a}_{2}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*}).

Define the intrinsic set

I=\{x\in\mathcal{S}:\operatorname{dist}(x,I_{\mathrm{sing}})<4t_{*}\}

and note that
I
is a
O(\rho)
-tubular neighborhood of
I_{\mathrm{sing}}
in
\mathcal{S}
, and hence the same volume argument from the bound on
b_{2}
applies, i.e.
Q_{*}(I)\lesssim\rho
. If we take
X\sim Q_{*}
and
X+Z_{*}\in V
with
\|Z_{*}\|<t_{*}
, then
\operatorname{dist}(X,I_{\mathrm{sing}})\leq\operatorname{dist}(X+Z_{*},I_{\mathrm{sing}})+\|Z_{*}\|<4t_{*}
, which means that
X\in I
. Hence

P_{*}(V)=\int_{I}\mathbb{P}(X+Z_{*}\in V)Q_{*}(dx)+\int_{\mathcal{S}\setminus I}\mathbb{P}(X+Z_{*}\in V)Q_{*}(dx)\leq Q_{*}(I)+\mathbb{P}(\|Z_{*}\|\geq t_{*})\lesssim\rho+\epsilon.

With the Hellinger bound, we have that
P_{f,\sigma}(V)\leq P_{*}(V)+\sqrt{2}\epsilon
, and so

\tilde{a}_{2}\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})\lesssim\rho+\epsilon,

and
\mathbb{P}(\|Z\|<t_{*})\geq 1/2
yields
\tilde{a}_{2}\lesssim\rho+\epsilon.

Hence

a_{2}=\tilde{a}_{2}+\hat{a}_{2}\lesssim\rho+\epsilon\lesssim\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}+\epsilon.

Bounding
|a_{1}-b_{1}|
and
|a_{2}-b_{2}|
.

Note that
a_{1}+a_{2}=b_{1}+b_{2}=1
, so

|a_{1}-b_{1}|=|a_{2}-b_{2}|\leq a_{2}+b_{2}\lesssim\rho+\epsilon.

Hence

\frac{R}{2}(|a_{1}-b_{1}|+|a_{2}-b_{2}|)\lesssim\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}.

Bounding
W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(1)},Q_{*}^{(1)})
.

The assumption that
\rho\leq\rho_{1}
implies that
b_{1}\geq c
, and so

a_{1}\geq b_{1}-|a_{1}-b_{1}|\geq c-C_{0}(\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})\geq c/2

for some constant
C_{0}>0
as
\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
is small. This allows us to control the
L^{1}
distance on the restricted densities. Let
p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}=Q_{f}^{(1)}*\phi_{\sigma}
and
p_{*}^{(1)}=Q_{*}^{(1)}*\phi_{\sigma_{*}}
so that

p_{f,\sigma}=a_{1}p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}+a_{2}(Q_{f}^{(2)}*\phi_{\sigma}),\quad p_{*}=b_{1}p_{*}^{(1)}+b_{2}(Q_{*}^{(2)}*\phi_{\sigma_{*}}).

Rearranging gives us that

a_{1}p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-b_{1}p_{*}^{(1)}=(p_{f,\sigma}-p_{*})-(a_{2}Q_{f}^{(2)}*\phi_{\sigma}-b_{2}Q_{*}^{(2)}*\phi_{\sigma_{*}}).

Then

\|a_{1}p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-b_{1}p_{*}^{(1)}\|_{1}\leq\|p_{f,\sigma}-p_{*}\|_{1}+a_{2}+b_{2}.

Using the relation
p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-p_{*}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{a_{1}}(a_{1}p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-b_{1}p_{*}^{(1)})+\frac{b_{1}-a_{1}}{a_{1}}p_{*}^{(1)}
implies that

\|p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-p_{*}^{(1)}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{a_{1}}(\|p_{f,\sigma}-p_{*}\|_{1}+a_{2}+b_{2}+|a_{1}-b_{1}|)\leq\frac{2}{c}(2\sqrt{2}d_{H}(p_{f,\sigma},p_{*})+a_{2}+b_{2}+|a_{1}-b_{1}|).

By our assumption on the Hellinger distance and bounds in the previous steps, we have that each of the terms is
\lesssim\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
. Since
Q_{*}^{(1)}
is supported on the set
A
with
\operatorname{reach}(A)\geq 4t_{*}
, we can apply the argument in Theorem 7 in
12
to get
\sigma\leq 2t_{*}
and

\displaystyle W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(1)},Q_{*}^{(1)})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma+\|p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-p_{*}^{(1)}\|_{1}+\sigma_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{*}+\|p_{f,\sigma}^{(1)}-p_{*}^{(1)}\|_{1}\lesssim\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}.

Thus
b_{1}W_{1}(Q_{f}^{(1)},Q_{*}^{(1)})\lesssim\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
.

Putting all bounds together gives us that

W_{1}(Q_{f},Q_{*})\lesssim\epsilon+\sigma_{*}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}.

\hfill{\Box}

A.5
Proof of Lemma
19

Let
m=\lceil\log_{2}2\xi^{-p}\rceil
and
\eta=2^{-m}
. Consider the function

g_{c,\eta}(x)=\operatorname{ReLU}(x-c)-\operatorname{ReLU}(x-c-\eta),

and define
D(u,v)=(\operatorname{ReLU}(u+v),\operatorname{ReLU}(u+v))
. In particular, for
u=v\geq 0
, this gives
D(u,v)=(2u,2u)
. Set
(u_{0},v_{0})=(g_{c,\eta}(x),g_{c,\eta}(x))
and

(u_{i},v_{i})=D(u_{i-1},v_{i-1}).

Then
u_{m}=v_{m}=2^{m}g_{c,\eta}(x)
. Let
f_{c,\eta}(x)=u_{m}=2^{m}g_{c,\eta}(x)=\frac{1}{\eta}g_{c,\eta}(x)
. Then

f_{c,\eta}(x)=\begin{cases}0,&x\leq c;\\
\frac{x-c}{\eta},&c<x<c+\eta;\\
1,&x\geq c+\eta.\end{cases}

Set
\Phi(x)=f_{a-\eta,\eta}(x)-f_{b,\eta}(x)
. Then properties 1, 2, and 3 of the lemma are satisfied with the specified layers, width and sparsity.
\hfill{\Box}

A.6
Proof of Lemma
21

By the triangle inequality,

W_{1}(\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}Q_{k})\leq W_{1}(\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k})+W_{1}(\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}Q_{k}).

The second term on the right captures the error in the distribution estimation and can be bounded as follows. Take an optimal coupling of
\tilde{Q}_{k}
and
Q_{k}
for each
k
, say
\gamma_{k}
. Define a new measure
\gamma=\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\gamma_{k}
. This gives us marginals of
\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}Q_{k}
, so this is a coupling of the two mixtures. Thus, an optimal coupling would satisfy

W_{1}(\sum_{k}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k}b_{k}Q_{k})\leq\int|x-y|d\gamma(x,y)=\sum_{k}b_{k}\int|x-y|d\gamma_{k}(x,y)=\sum_{k}b_{k}W_{1}(\tilde{Q}_{k},Q_{k}).

The first term on the right of the beginning inequality captures the error in the estimation of the mixture weights. By (
51
Theorem 6.15),
W_{1}(\mu,\nu)\leq R||\mu-\nu||_{TV}
, where
R=diam(\mathcal{Y}_{*})
and
||\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k}||_{TV}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|a_{k}-b_{k}|
. Thus

W_{1}(\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k},\sum_{k=1}^{K}b_{k}\tilde{Q}_{k})\leq\frac{R}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|a_{k}-b_{k}|

Adding these bounds together gives the claim.
\hfill{\Box}

Appendix B
Proofs for Section 4

For the sake of simplicity and without loss of generality, in the following analysis, we assume
\mathcal{M}=\cup_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{M}_{k}\subset\mathbb{B}_{1}(0_{D})
. Recall that
\sigma_{t}=\sqrt{1-\exp(-2\int_{0}^{t}\beta_{s}\,{\rm d}s)}
and
m_{t}=\sqrt{1-\sigma_{t}^{2}}
. Then there exists a constant
C
so that
\frac{1}{c}\sqrt{t\wedge 1}\leq\sigma_{t}\leq c\sqrt{t\wedge 1}
and
\frac{1}{c}(t\wedge 1)\leq 1-m_{t}\leq c(t\wedge 1)
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)\propto\mathbb{E}_{y\sim P_{*}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\propto\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}z\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\big)\exp\big(-\frac{\|y-z\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}z\Big]

\displaystyle\propto\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\exp\bigg(-\Big(\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}\Big)\Big\|z-\frac{\frac{m_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}x+\frac{y}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}}{\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{*}^{2}}}\Big\|^{2}\bigg)\,{\rm d}z\cdot\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2(m_{t}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2})}\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\propto\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Bigg[\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2(m_{t}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2})}\bigg)\Bigg].

So let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}=\sqrt{m_{t}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2}}
, we can write

p_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Bigg[\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg],

and

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}=\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]}.

We first use Lemma B.2 in
49
to relate the generalization error of the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})
to the generalization error of the distribution
P_{*}
under the
W_{1}
metric

Lemma 23
.

(Lemma B.2 of
49
)
Suppose
\widehat{S}(x,t)\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sigma_{t}}
, then

W_{1}\left(\widehat{p},P_{*}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sum_{i=0}^{L-1}\sqrt{\big((t_{i}\log n)\wedge 1\big)\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t}.

Moreover, when
S(x_{t},t)\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{t\wedge 1}}
, we have for any
l\in\{0,1,\cdots,L-1\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\|S(x_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x_{t}|x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x_{t}|x)\,{\rm d}{x_{t}}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int 2\cdot\|S(x_{t},t)\|^{2}p_{t}(x_{t}|x)\,{\rm d}{x_{t}}{\rm d}t+\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int 2\cdot\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x_{t}|x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x_{t}|x)\,{\rm d}{x_{t}}{\rm d}t\lesssim\log^{2}n.

Then by Theorem 4.3 of
37
, for any
l\in\{0,1,\cdots,L-1\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{S\in\Phi(L_{l},W_{l},R_{l},B_{l},V_{l})}\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|S(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}R_{l}L_{l}\log\left(nL_{l}\left\|W_{l}\right\|_{\infty}B_{l}\right).

We will choose
R_{l},L_{l}
,
\left\|W_{l}\right\|_{\infty}
and
B_{l}
so that the approximation error satisfies

\inf_{S\in\Phi(L_{l},W_{l},R_{l},B_{l},V_{l})}\int_{t_{l}}^{t_{l+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t=\widetilde{O}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min\Big(\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{l}})^{-d_{k}}}{n},n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\Big)\bigg).

We first introduce the following lemma, which states that it is sufficient to approximate the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
only for values of
x
that are in close proximity to
\mathcal{M}=\cup_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{M}_{k}
.

Lemma 24
.

If
\underset{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}{\sup}\underset{t\in[\tau,T]}{\sup}[\|S(x,t)\|_{\infty}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}]\leq c{\sqrt{\log n}}
. Then, there exist constants
(c_{0},c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})
so that for any
i\in\{0,1,\cdots,L-1\}
and
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
with
1<\frac{t_{i+1}}{t_{i}}\leq 2
,

1.

Denote
{\operatorname{dist}}(x,\mathcal{M})
as the distance of point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
to
\mathcal{M}
. Then

\displaystyle\int\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-S(x,t)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-S(x,t)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x+(1+c^{2})\cdot c_{1}\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

2.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
satisfying
{\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}
, we have

(a)

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}
.

(b)

(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}p_{t}(x)\geq n^{-c_{3}}
.

Furthermore, we will use Lemma C.2 of
49
to bound the covering number of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
.

Lemma 25
.

(Lemma C.2 of
49
)
For any
k\in[K]
and any
\epsilon>0
, there exists an
\epsilon
-cover
N_{k,\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
so that
N_{k,\epsilon}\subset\mathcal{M}_{k}
and
|N_{k,\epsilon}|\lesssim(\epsilon\wedge 1)^{-d_{k}}
, moreover, for any
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
and
r\geq\epsilon
, we have

\big|\{x\in N_{k,\epsilon}\,:\,\|x-x_{0}\|\leq r\}\big|\lesssim\big(\frac{r\wedge 1}{\epsilon\wedge 1}\big)^{d_{k}}.

Let us fix an
i\in\{0,1,\cdots,L-1\}
and time interval
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
where
1<\frac{t_{i+1}}{t_{i}}\leq 2
,
t_{i}\geq\tau
and
t_{i+1}\leq T
. Using Lemma
24
, we only need to control

\displaystyle\inf_{S\in\Phi(L_{l},W_{l},R_{l},B_{l},V_{l})}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|S(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\cdot\inf_{S\in\Phi(L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i},V_{i})}\sup_{t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]}\sup_{x\,:\,\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\left\|S(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}.

Depending on the value of
t_{i}
and
\sigma_{*}
, we will control the above term using different methods.

B.1
Case 1: for any
k\in[K]
,
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\geq\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}

For any
k\in[K]
, let
N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}
be an
\epsilon^{*}
-cover of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
with
\epsilon^{*}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
so that statements in Lemma
25
are satisfied. Then the cardinality of
N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}
, denoted by
|N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}|
, satisfies
|N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}|=\mathcal{O}\big(1\vee(\epsilon^{*})^{-d_{k}}\big)
. Denote
J_{k}=|N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}|
and write
N_{k,\epsilon^{*}}=\{x_{k,1}^{*},x_{k,2}^{*},\cdots,x_{k,J_{k}}^{*}\}
. For ease of notation, we denote

(A)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]

and

(B)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big],

so that
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=\frac{(A)}{(B)}
. Then using Lemma
24
, we have for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
satisfying
{\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}
, it holds that
(B)\geq n^{-c_{3}}
and
\|\frac{(A)}{(B)}\|\leq c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}
. Define the following partition function

\rho(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1&|x|<1\\
0&|x|>2\\
2-|x|&1<|x|\leq 2.\end{array}\right.

Let
c_{4}
be a large enough constant, we approximate
(A)
by

(A^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)},

and approximate
(B)
by

(B^{\prime})=\max\bigg(n^{-c_{3}},\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}\bigg).

Then with sufficiently large
c_{4}
, for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
,

\displaystyle\|(A)-(A^{\prime})\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho(\frac{|x-x_{kj}^{*}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}})<1\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}-1\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big(\forall j,\,\|x-x_{kj}^{*}\|\geq\frac{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{D}}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big({\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})\geq\big(\frac{c_{4}}{\sqrt{D}}-1\big)\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c_{3}-1},

and similarly

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|(B)-(B^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big({\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})\geq\big(\frac{c_{4}}{\sqrt{D}}-1\big)\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c_{3}-1}.

Combined with
(B)\geq n^{-c_{3}}
and , we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-\frac{(A^{\prime})}{(B^{\prime})}\Big\|=\Big\|\frac{(A)}{(B)}-\frac{(A^{\prime})}{(B^{\prime})}\Big\|\leq\Big\|\frac{(A)}{(B)}\Big\|\cdot\frac{|(B)-(B^{\prime})|}{|(B^{\prime})|}+\frac{\|(A)-(A^{\prime})\|}{|B^{\prime}|}\lesssim n^{-1}.

Then for any
k\in[K]
,
j\in[J_{k}]
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
\|x-x_{kj}^{*}\|\leq 2c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
, we have

\displaystyle\{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\,:\,\|x-y\|\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

\displaystyle\subset\{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\,:\,\|y-x_{kj}^{*}\|\leq(2c_{4}+c_{5})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}.\,

Let
c_{6}=2c_{4}+c_{5}
, we further approximate
(A^{\prime})
by

(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)},

and approximate
(B^{\prime})
by

(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})=\max\bigg(n^{-c_{3}},\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}\bigg).

When
c_{5}
is sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\|(A^{\prime})-(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\cdot q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\cdot q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\cdot q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c_{3}-1},

and similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|(B^{\prime})-(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c_{3}-1}.

Therefore, combined with
(B^{\prime\prime})\geq(B)-2n^{-c_{3}-1}\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}\geq\frac{n^{-c_{3}}}{2}
and
\|\frac{(A^{\prime})}{(B^{\prime})}\|\leq\|\frac{(A)}{(B)}\|+n^{-1}\leq 2c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}
, we have

\Big\|\frac{(A^{\prime})}{(B^{\prime})}-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}\Big\|\leq\Big\|\frac{(A^{\prime})}{(B^{\prime})}\Big\|\cdot\frac{|(B^{\prime})-(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})|}{|(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})|}+\frac{\|(A^{\prime})-(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})\|}{|B^{{}^{\prime\prime}}|}\lesssim n^{-1}.

Then let
\mathscr{L}=c_{7}\log n
with a sufficiently large
c_{7}
and consider

\displaystyle(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}\big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l+1}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l+1}\omega_{k}(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}})^{2l+2}\cdot m_{t}^{s_{1}}\cdot x^{s_{2}}\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\cdot a_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)},

where for
s_{2}=(s_{21},s_{22},\cdots,s_{2D})
, we denote
x^{s_{2}}=\prod_{j=1}^{D}x_{j}^{s_{2j}}
, and
a_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}
’s are
D
-dimensional coefficients that satisfy
(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}})^{2l+2}\|a_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}\|=\exp(\mathcal{O}(\log^{2}n))
. Similarly, we define

\displaystyle(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})=\max\bigg(n^{-c_{3}},\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\max\bigg(n^{-c_{3}},\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l}\omega_{k}(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}})^{2l}\cdot m_{t}^{s_{1}}\cdot x^{s_{2}}\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\cdot b_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\right)}\bigg),

where
b_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}
’s are
1
-dimensional coefficients that satisfy
(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}})^{2l}|b_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}|=\exp(\mathcal{O}(\log^{2}n))
. Using the fact that for any
x\geq 0
, it holds that
|\exp(-x)-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{x^{l}}{l!}|\leq\frac{|x|^{\mathscr{L}+1}}{(\mathscr{L}+1)!}\leq(\frac{|x|e}{(\mathscr{L}+1)})^{\mathscr{L}+1}
. When
\mathscr{L}=c_{7}\log n
with a sufficiently large
c_{7}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})-(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Bigg(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\Big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\Big|\exp(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}})-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}\Big|\cdot\big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Bigg(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\Big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{(c_{6}+2c_{4})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\Big|\exp(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}})-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}\Big|\cdot\big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\sup_{k\in[K]}\sup_{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{(c_{6}+2c_{4})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\Big|\exp(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}})-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}\Big|(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}))^{d_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\sup_{k\in[K]}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}))^{d_{k}}\cdot\sup_{0\leq z\leq(c_{6}+2c_{4})^{2}\log n+1}\Big|\exp(-z)-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\frac{(-z)^{l}}{l!}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\sup_{k\in[K]}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}))^{d_{k}}\cdot\big(e\frac{(c_{6}+2c_{4})^{2}\log n+1}{c_{7}\log n+1}\big)^{c_{7}\log n+1}\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1},

and similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})-(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Bigg(\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\Big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\cap\mathbb{B}_{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x_{kj}^{*})}\Big|\exp(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}})-\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}(-1)^{l}\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2l}}{l!2^{l}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2l}}\Big|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\sup_{k\in[K]}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}))^{d_{k}}\cdot\big(e\frac{(c_{6}+2c_{4})^{2}\log n+1}{c_{7}\log n+1}\big)^{c_{7}\log n+1}\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1}.

So we have

\Big\|\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}\Big\|\lesssim n^{-1}.

Now we will construct neural network to approximate
\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}
. We consider the following lemmas in
37
for the approximation of
m_{t}
,
\sigma_{t}
, monomial and reciprocal function. In what follows, we write
\Phi(L,W,R,B)=\Phi(L,W,R,B,\infty)
.

Lemma 26
.

(Lemma 3.3 in
37
) There exist neural networks
\phi_{m}(t),\phi_{\sigma}(t)\in\Phi(L,W,B,R)
that approximates
m_{t}
and
\sigma_{t}
up to
\varepsilon
for all
t\geq 0
, where
L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right)\right)
,
\|W\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right)\right)
,
R=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right)\right)
, and
B=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right)\right)\right)
.

Lemma 27
.

(Lemma F.6 in
37
) Let
d\geq 2,C\geq 1,0<\varepsilon_{\text{error }}\leq 1
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a neural network
\phi_{\text{mult}}\left(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{d}\right)\in\Phi(L,W,R,B)
with
L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log d\left(\log\varepsilon^{-1}+d\log C\right)\right),\|W\|_{\infty}=48d
,
R=\mathcal{O}\left(d\log\varepsilon^{-1}+d\log C\right),B=C^{d}
such that for all
x\in[-C,C]^{d}
and
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\left\|x-x^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\text{error}}
,

\left|\phi_{\text{mult}}\left(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime},\cdots,x_{d}^{\prime}\right)-\prod_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}x_{d^{\prime}}\right|\leq\varepsilon+dC^{d-1}\varepsilon_{\text{error}},

and for all
x\in[-C,C]
,
|\phi_{\text{mult}}(x)|\leq C^{d}
Note that some of
x_{i},x_{j}(i\neq j)
can be shared. For
\prod_{i=1}^{I}x_{i}^{\omega_{i}}
with
\omega_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}(i=1,2,\cdots,I)
and
\sum_{i=1}^{I}\omega_{i}=d
, there exists a neural network satisfying the same bounds as above, and the network is denoted by
\phi_{\text{mult}}(x;\omega)
.

Lemma 28
.

(Lemma F.7 in
37
) For any
0<\varepsilon<1
, there exists
\phi_{\text{rec}}\in\Phi(L,W,R,B)
with
L\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}\right),\|W\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}\right),R=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}\right)
, and
B=\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{-2}\right)
such that

\left|\phi_{\text{rec}}\left(x^{\prime}\right)-\frac{1}{x}\right|\leq\varepsilon+\frac{\left|x^{\prime}-x\right|}{\varepsilon^{2}},\quad\text{ for all }x\in\left[\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{-1}\right]\text{ and }x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.

Therefore, using Lemmas
26
,
30
,
27
and
28
, we

1.

Approximate
m_{t}
by
\phi_{m}(t)\in\Phi(L_{1}^{\prime},W_{1}^{\prime},R_{1}^{\prime},B_{1}^{\prime})
with
L_{1}^{\prime}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
,
\|W_{1}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log^{6}n)
,
R^{\prime}_{1}=\Theta(\log^{8}n)
and
B_{1}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{4}n))
.

2.

Approximate
\sigma_{t}
by
\phi_{\sigma}(t)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{2},W^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2},B^{\prime}_{2})
with
L^{\prime}_{2}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{2}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log^{6}n)
,
R^{\prime}_{2}=\Theta(\log^{8}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{2}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{4}n))
.

3.

Approximate
\frac{1}{x}
by
\phi_{rec}(x)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{3},W^{\prime}_{3},R^{\prime}_{3},B^{\prime}_{3})
with
L^{\prime}_{3}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{3}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log^{6}n)
,
R^{\prime}_{3}=\Theta(\log^{8}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{3}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{4}n))
.

4.

For vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}^{D}
, approximate
x^{s}
by
\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{4},W^{\prime}_{4},R^{\prime}_{4},B^{\prime}_{4})
with

L^{\prime}_{4}=\Theta(\log^{2}n\cdot\log\log n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{4}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log n)
,
R^{\prime}_{4}=\Theta(\log^{3}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{4}=\exp(\Theta(\log n\cdot\log\log n))
.

5.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}
, Approximate
x^{a}
by
\phi_{power}(x;a)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{5},W^{\prime}_{5},R^{\prime}_{5},B^{\prime}_{5})
with
L^{\prime}_{5}=\Theta(\log^{2}n\cdot\log\log n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{5}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log n)
,
R^{\prime}_{5}=\Theta(\log^{3}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{5}=\exp(\Theta(\log n\cdot\log\log n))
.

6.

For
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, Approximate
x\cdot y
by
\phi_{mult}(x,y)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{6},W^{\prime}_{6},R^{\prime}_{6},B^{\prime}_{6})
with
L^{\prime}_{6}=\Theta(\log^{2}n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{6}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(1)
,
R^{\prime}_{6}=\Theta(\log^{2}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{6}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{2}n))
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, we denote
\phi_{mult}(x,y)=(\phi_{mult}(x,y_{1}),\phi_{mult}(x,y_{2}),\cdots,\phi_{mult}(x,y_{D}))
.

Then we denote
\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t)=\phi_{rec}(\sigma_{*}^{2}\cdot\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),2)+\phi_{power}(\phi_{\sigma}(t),2))
and
\phi_{\rho}(x;x^{*},a)=\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}|}{a}\big)
. Using the fact that
\rho(x)={\rm ReLU}(2-|x|)-{\rm ReLU}(1-|x|)
and
|x|={\rm ReLU}(x)+{\rm ReLU}(-x)
,
\phi_{\rho}(x;x^{*},a)
can be realized exactly by ReLU neural network. We construct

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l+1}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l+1}\omega_{k}\cdot a_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}\cdot\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l+1),

\displaystyle\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{2})\Big),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\bigg)\Bigg),

and

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\max\Bigg(n^{-c_{3}},\sum_{k=1}^{K}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l}\omega_{k}\cdot b_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}\cdot\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l),

\displaystyle\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{2})\Big),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\bigg)\Bigg)\Bigg).

Then using Lemmas
26
,
27
and
28
, we can get

\|\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)-(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1},

and

\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)-(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1}.

So

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}\Big\|+\Big\|\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}\Big\|+\Big\|\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime\prime}})}-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime}})}\Big\|+\Big\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-\frac{(A^{{}^{\prime}})}{(B^{{}^{\prime}})}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1}.

Define

\phi^{*}(x,t)=\max\bigg(-c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}},\min\bigg(c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}},\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{rec}\big(\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)\big),\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)\Big)\bigg)\bigg).

Since
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\leq c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}
, it holds for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{*}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{rec}\big(\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)\big),\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)\Big)-\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}\Big\|+\Big\|\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\Big\|\lesssim n^{-1}.

Furthermore, note that
\max(x,a)={\rm ReLU}(x-a)+a
and
\min(x,a)=-\big({\rm ReLU}(-x+a)-a\big)
, based on Lemmas F.1-F.3 in
37
for the concatenation and parallelization of neural networks, there exists
L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i}
with
L_{i}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
,
\|W_{i}\|_{\infty}=\Theta\big(\log^{6}n+\sum_{k=1}^{K}J_{k}(\log n)^{D+2})
,
R_{i}=\Theta\big(\log^{8}n+\sum_{k=1}^{K}J_{k}(\log n)^{D+4})
, and
B_{i}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{4}n))
so that
\phi^{*}\in\Phi(L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i},c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}})
. So we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{S\in\Phi(L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i},V_{i})}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|S(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}R_{i}L_{i}\log\left(nL_{i}\left\|W_{i}\right\|_{\infty}B_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\cdot\sup_{t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]}\sup_{x\,:\,\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\left\|\phi^{*}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}R_{i}L_{i}\log\left(nL_{i}\left\|W_{i}\right\|_{\infty}B_{i}\right)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n}\Big).

Then note that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\geq\max_{k\in[K]}\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}
and

\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{2}=m_{t_{i}}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}+\sigma_{t_{i}}^{2}\geq m_{t_{i}}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}\mathbb{1}(t_{i}<\frac{1}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{t_{i}}^{2}\mathbb{1}(t_{i}\geq\frac{1}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{t_{i}}^{2}\gtrsim\sigma_{*}^{2}+1\wedge t_{i},

where we have used that
\sigma_{*}\leq 1
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n}\Big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n})\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[((t_{i}\log n)\wedge 1)\cdot\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}})^{-d_{k}+2}}{n}\Big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{1}{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}+2}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{\sigma_{*}^{2-d_{k}}}{n})\Big).

B.2
Case 2:
\exists\,k\in[K]
,
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}<\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}
.

Consider the sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}=\{k\in[K]\,:\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\geq\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{2}=\{k\in[K]\,:\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}<\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}\}.

Then we write

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]}_{(A_{1})}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]}_{(A_{2})},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]}_{(B_{1})}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]}_{(B_{2})}

Then similar as case 1, we can approximate terms
(A_{1})
and
(B_{1})
by

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(A_{1})}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l+1}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l+1}\omega_{k}\cdot a_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}\cdot\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l+1),

\displaystyle\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{2})\Big),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\bigg)\Bigg),

and

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(B_{1})}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{1}}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\mathscr{L}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{2}|\leq 2l}\omega_{k}\cdot b_{k,j,l,s_{1},s_{2}}\cdot\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l),

\displaystyle\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{2})\Big),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n})\bigg)\Bigg),

respectively. Furthermore, it holds for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\sqrt{\log n}
that

\|\phi^{*}_{(A_{1})}(x,t)-(A)\|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1},

and

\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)-(B)|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1}.

Then we approximate terms
(A_{2})
and
(B_{2})
. For any
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
, let
\widetilde{N}_{k,\epsilon^{*}_{k}}
be an
\epsilon^{*}_{k}
-cover of
\mathcal{M}_{k}
with
\epsilon^{*}_{k}=n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
so that statements in Lemma
25
are satisfied. Then
|\widetilde{N}_{k,\epsilon^{*}_{k}}|=\mathcal{O}\big(n^{\frac{d_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\big)
. Denote
\widetilde{J}_{k}=|\widetilde{N}_{k,\epsilon^{*}_{k}}|
and write
\widetilde{N}_{k,\epsilon^{*}_{k}}=\{\widetilde{x}_{k,1}^{*},\widetilde{x}_{k,2}^{*},\cdots,\widetilde{x}_{k,\widetilde{J}_{k}}^{*}\}
. Then define

(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)},

and

(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}.

With sufficiently large
c_{4}
, for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
,

\displaystyle\|(A_{2})-(A^{\prime}_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\leq 1\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}-1\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big(\forall j,\,\|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}\|\geq\frac{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}{\sqrt{D}}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big({\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})\geq\big(\frac{c_{4}}{\sqrt{D}}-1\big)n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{1}\Big({\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})\geq\big(\frac{c_{4}}{\sqrt{D}}-1\big)\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c_{3}-1},

and similarly

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|(B_{2})-(B^{\prime}_{2})|\leq n^{-c_{3}-1}.

Then since for any
k\in[K]
,
\mathcal{M}_{k}
is
\beta_{k}
-smooth, there exists a positive constant
r
so that for any
y^{*}\in\mathcal{M}_{k}
,

1.

The projection function
{\rm Proj}_{T_{y^{*}}\mathcal{M}}(x-y^{*})
is a local diffeomorphism in
y^{*}
, with the inverse
\Psi_{y^{*}}
defined on
\mathbb{B}_{r}(\mathbb{0}_{D})\cap T_{y^{*}}\mathcal{M}
and is
\beta
-smooth.

2.

\mathbb{B}_{r}(y^{*})\cap\mathcal{M}\subset\Psi_{y^{*}}(B_{r}(\mathbb{0}_{D})\cap T_{y^{*}}\mathcal{M})\subset\mathbb{B}_{8r/7}(y^{*})\cap\mathcal{M}
.

Then for any
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]
, let
V_{kj}
be an arbitrary orthonormal basis for the tangent space
T_{\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}}\mathcal{M}_{k}
at
\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}
. Define a function
G^{*}_{kj}
with domain
\mathbb{B}_{r}(0_{d_{k}})
so that

G^{*}_{kj}(z)=\Psi_{\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}}(V_{kj}z)

(B.1)

Then we can define the local inverse function of
G^{*}_{kj}

Q^{*}_{kj}(y)=V_{kj}^{T}\cdot{\rm Proj}_{T_{\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}}\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj})=V_{kj}^{T}(y-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}).

(B.2)

For any
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
, and
j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]
, consider the Taylor expansion of
G^{*}_{kj}
at
0_{d_{k}}
,

G^{*}_{kj}(z)=G_{kj}(z)+O(\|z\|^{\beta_{k}}),

where

G_{kj}(z)=\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}+\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\atop 1\leq|s|\leq\lfloor\beta_{k}\rfloor}G^{*}_{kj}{}^{(s)}(0_{d_{k}})\cdot z^{s}

(B.3)

is the polynomial approximation to
G^{*}_{kj}
. For any
z\in\mathbb{B}_{r}(0_{d_{k}})
, it holds that
\|G^{*}_{kj}(z)-G_{kj}(z)\|\lesssim\|z\|^{\beta_{k}}
and
\|\nabla G^{*}_{kj}(z)-\nabla G_{kj}(z)\|\lesssim\|z\|^{\beta_{k}-1}
, where
\nabla G(z)=(\nabla G_{1}(z),\nabla G_{2}(z),\cdots,\nabla G_{D}(z))^{T}
is the Jacobian matrix of
G=(G_{1},G_{2},\cdots,G_{D})
. Then we use the following lemma to approximate the projection function
{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)
for
x
near
x_{kj}^{*}
, the proof of which directly follows from Lemma C.8 of
49
.

Lemma 29
.

(Lemma C.8 of
49
)
For any
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]
, there exists a neural network
\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)\in\Phi(L^{\prime}_{kj},W^{\prime}_{kj},R^{\prime}_{kj},B^{\prime}_{kj})
with
L^{\prime}_{kj}=\Theta(\log^{2}n)
,
\|W^{\prime}_{kj}\|_{\infty}=\Theta(\log^{3}n)
,
R^{\prime}_{kj}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
and
B^{\prime}_{kj}=\exp(\Theta(\log n))
so that for any
x
with
\|x-x_{kj}^{*}\|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
,

1.

\Big\|\big\langle\nabla G_{kj}(\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)),x-G_{jk}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))\big\rangle\Big\|\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
.

2.

\big\|\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)-Q^{*}_{kj}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x))\big\|\lesssim n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
.

Then we define

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big),

and

\displaystyle(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big).

Then

\displaystyle\big\|(A_{2}^{\prime})-(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\Big]

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\cdot\Big|1-\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{J_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|\geq c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\atop|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\Big]}_{(C)}

\displaystyle+\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)|\leq 2c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\atop|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}{\sup}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\underbrace{\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y).\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad}_{(D)}

We first bound term
(C)
. Note that by Lemma
29
, we have for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}
,

\|\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)\|\leq\|\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)-Q^{*}_{kj}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}x)\|+\|{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}\|\lesssim n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})},

and there exist constants
C,C_{1}
so that

\displaystyle|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|+|G^{*}_{jk}(Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)))-G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|+|G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-G_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|+C\,n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}

\displaystyle\leq|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|+C_{1}\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\tau}\leq|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|+C_{1}\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}.

Hence when
c_{6}
is large enough,

\displaystyle(C)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|\geq(c_{6}-C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})\geq\frac{(c_{6}-C_{1})}{\sqrt{D}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sup}\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[2\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-y\|^{2}}{4\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big\|1+\frac{x-y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|\Big]\leq\frac{n^{-c_{3}-1}}{2}.

Then we bound term
(D)
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)|\leq 2c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
and
|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}
, we have

\displaystyle|x-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|+|G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-G_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|+|G^{*}_{jk}(Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}x))-G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|+C_{1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}

\displaystyle\{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\,:\,\|y-x\|\leq c\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

\displaystyle\subset\{y\in\mathcal{M}\,:\,\|y-{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)\|\leq(c+2c_{6}+C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

\displaystyle\subset\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x))\|\leq(c+2c_{6}+C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

Furthermore, using Lemma
29
and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\geq\widetilde{\sigma}_{\tau}\gtrsim\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\min_{k\in[K]}\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\geq\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\min_{k\in[K]}\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|z-\phi^{p}_{jk}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|z-Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x))\|+\|\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq\|z-Q^{*}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}}(x))\|+Cn^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d}}\leq\|z-Q^{*}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}}(x))\|+C_{1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}},

and thus

\displaystyle\{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\,:\,\|y-x\|\leq c\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}\subset\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-Q^{*}_{jk}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x))\|\leq(c+2c_{6}+C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

\displaystyle\subset\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi^{p}_{jk}(x)\|\leq(c+2c_{6}+2C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}

\displaystyle\subset\{y=G^{*}_{jk}(z)\,:\,\|z-\phi^{p}_{jk}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq(c+2c_{6}+2C_{1})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}.

So when
c_{5}
is sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle(D)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)|\leq 2c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\atop|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}{\sup}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\frac{c_{5}}{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big\|\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sup_{j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]}\underset{x\,:\,|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)|\leq 2c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\atop|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}{\sup}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\frac{c_{5}}{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}(x)}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-y\|^{2}}{4\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big\|1+\frac{x-y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big\|q_{k}(y)\,{\rm d}{\rm vol}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{-c_{3}-1}}{2}.

So
\|(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime}})-(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})\|\leq n^{-c_{3}-1}
. Similarly, we can show that
\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime}})-(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})|\leq n^{-c_{3}-1}
. Then denote
v^{*}_{kj}(z)=q_{k}(G^{*}_{kj}(z))\sqrt{\operatorname{det}\big(\nabla G^{*}_{kj}(z)^{T}\nabla G^{*}_{kj}(z)\big)}
, we can rewrite term
(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})
as

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)v^{*}_{kj}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)

For any
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
, and
j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]
, consider the Taylor expansion of
v^{*}_{kj}
at
0_{d_{k}}
,

v^{*}_{kj}(z)=v_{kj}(z)+O(\|z\|^{\alpha_{k}}),

where

v_{kj}(z)=v^{*}_{kj}(0_{d_{k}})+\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\atop 1\leq|s|\leq\lfloor\alpha_{k}\rfloor}v*_{kj}{}^{(s)}(0_{d_{k}})\cdot z^{s}.

(B.4)

We define

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}
