\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)v_{kj}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)

and

\displaystyle(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{\omega_{k}}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot v_{kj}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\cdot\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big).

Then we have

\displaystyle\big\|(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})-(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\big\|\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\frac{\omega_{k}\rho\big(\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\rho\big(\frac{|x-G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))|}{c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\big)}{\max\left(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\rho\big(\frac{|x-x^{*}_{kj}|}{c_{4}n^{-{1}/(2\alpha_{k}+d_{k})}}\big)\right)}\cdot\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot(E),

\displaystyle\text{ where }(E)=\Bigg\|\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\big(-\frac{\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\rangle}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)v_{kj}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\big(-\frac{\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\rangle}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)v^{*}_{kj}(z)\,{\rm d}z\Bigg\|.

To bound the term
(E)
, consider an arbitrary
k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
j\in[\widetilde{J}_{k}]
, we will consider and bound the following terms using Lemma
29
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
|x-\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}|\leq 2c_{4}n^{\frac{-1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
and
|x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))|\leq 2c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
, and any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}
satisfying
\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
:

(i).\,\left\|G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\right\|\leq\left\||G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-G_{jk}(z)\right\|+(1-m_{t})\|G_{jk}(z)\|\lesssim\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}.

\displaystyle(ii).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\cdot\Big|\|G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}-\|G_{kj}(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\asymp\frac{\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}},

where we have used
\|\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|\lesssim n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
and
\|z\|\leq\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|+\|\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|\lesssim n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}
.

(iii).\,\|x-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|\leq\|x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))\|+\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|\lesssim\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}.

\displaystyle(iv).

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\cdot\Big|\big\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\big\rangle-\big\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\asymp\frac{\sqrt{\log n}\cdot n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}.

\displaystyle(v).\,\big|\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\rangle\big|

(B.5)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),\nabla G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)-z)\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-G_{kj}(z)-\nabla G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))(\phi^{p}_{kj}(x)-z)\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\langle x-G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),G_{kj}(z)-m_{t}G_{kj}(z)\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{3}(\log n)^{\frac{3}{2}}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{3}\sqrt{\log n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{3}(\log n)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Combining all the pieces, we can obtain

\displaystyle(E)\lesssim\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}z

(B.6)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\frac{n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\log n}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{t_{i}}}+\frac{n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\Big).

We then bound
\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}z
. Notice that

\displaystyle\|\phi^{p}_{jk}(x)-z\|

\displaystyle\leq\|G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{jk}(x))-G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|G_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{jk}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|+(1-m_{t})\|G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|+\|G_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{jk}(x))-G^{*}_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{jk}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq\|G_{jk}(\phi^{p}_{jk}(x))-m_{t}G^{*}_{jk}(z)\|+o(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}),

we have

\displaystyle\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq c_{5}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\phi^{p}_{jk}(x)-z\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\right)\,{\rm d}z\lesssim\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{d_{k}}.

So we can get

\displaystyle\big\|(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime}})-(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\big\|\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}

\displaystyle\cdot\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{d_{k}}\cdot\Big(\frac{n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\log n}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{t_{i}}}+\frac{n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}\Big).

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}\,:\,\|z-\phi_{jk}^{p}(x)\|\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|G_{kj}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x))-m_{t}G_{jk}(z)\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

Lemma 30
.

(Lemma F.12 in
37
) Take

arbitrarily. There exists a neural network

\varepsilon

.

B^{\prime}_{1}=\exp(\Theta(\log n\cdot\log\log n))
. Furthermore, we define

\phi_{G_{kj}}(z)=\widetilde{x}_{kj}^{*}+\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d_{k}}\atop 1\leq|s|\leq\lfloor\beta_{k}\rfloor}\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi^{[d_{k}]}_{vpower}(z;s),G^{*}_{kj}{}^{(s)}(0_{d_{k}})\Big).

We construct

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)=\phi_{(A_{1})}^{*}(x,t)+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\sum_{l_{1}=0}^{\mathscr{L}_{1}}\sum_{l_{2}=0}^{\mathscr{L}_{2}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l_{1}+l_{2}+1}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d_{k}},|s_{2}|\leq(2l_{1}+2l_{2}+1)\lfloor\beta_{k}\rfloor+d_{k}+\lfloor\alpha_{k}\rfloor}\sum_{s_{3}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{3}|\leq l_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot a^{\prime}_{k,j,l_{1},l_{2},s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}}

\displaystyle\cdot\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l_{1}+l_{2}+1),\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),

\displaystyle\phi^{[d_{k}]}_{vpower}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x);s_{2})\Big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{3})\bigg),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}})\bigg),\phi_{\rho}\big(x;\phi_{G_{kj}}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\big)\Bigg)\Bigg),

and

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\max\Bigg(\frac{n^{-c_{3}}}{4},\phi_{(B_{1})}^{*}(x,t)+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{rec}\Big(\max\big(1,\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\big)\Big),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\widetilde{J}_{k}}\sum_{l_{1}=0}^{\mathscr{L}_{1}}\sum_{l_{2}=0}^{\mathscr{L}_{2}}\sum_{0\leq s_{1}\leq 2l_{1}+l_{2}}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d_{k}},|s_{2}|\leq(2l_{1}+2l_{2})\lfloor\beta_{k}\rfloor+d_{k}+\lfloor\alpha_{k}\rfloor}\sum_{s_{3}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{D},|s_{3}|\leq l_{2}}\omega_{k}\cdot b^{\prime}_{k,j,l_{1},l_{2},s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}}

\displaystyle\cdot\phi_{mult}\Bigg(\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\bigg(\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{mult}\big(\phi_{power}(\phi_{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}}}(t),l_{1}+l_{2}),\phi_{power}(\phi_{m}(t),s_{1})\big),

\displaystyle\phi^{[d_{k}]}_{vpower}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x);s_{2})\Big),\phi^{[D]}_{vpower}(x;s_{3})\bigg),\phi_{\rho}(x;x_{kj}^{*},c_{4}n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}})\bigg),\phi_{\rho}\big(x;\phi_{G_{kj}}(\phi_{kj}^{p}(x)),c_{6}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\big)\Bigg)\Bigg)\Bigg).

Then using Lemmas
26
,
27
,
28
and
30
, as well as
\phi^{*}_{(B_{1})}(x,t)+(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\geq\frac{n^{-c_{3}}}{4}
, we can get

\|\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)-\phi_{(A_{1})}^{*}(x,t)-(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})\|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1},

and

\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}|\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)-\phi_{(B_{1})}^{*}(x,t)-(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})|\lesssim n^{-c_{3}-1}.

So

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)}{\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)}-\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A_{1})}(x,t)+(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{\phi^{*}_{(B_{1})}(x,t)+(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}\Big\|+\Big\|\frac{(A_{1})+(A_{2})}{(B_{1})+(B_{2})}-\frac{\phi^{*}_{(A_{1})}(x,t)+(A_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}{\phi^{*}_{(B_{1})}(x,t)+(B_{2}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}})}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\log n}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{t_{i}}}+\frac{n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}.

Finally, define

\phi^{*}(x,t)=\max\bigg(-c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}},\min\bigg(c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}},\phi_{mult}\Big(\phi_{rec}\big(\phi^{*}_{(B)}(x,t)\big),\phi^{*}_{(A)}(x,t)\Big)\bigg)\bigg).

Since
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\leq c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}
, it holds for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{*}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\big\|\lesssim\sup_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\log n}{\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}_{t_{i}}}+\frac{n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d}}\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}.

Furthermore, based on Lemmas F.1-F.3 in
37
for the concatenation and parallelization of neural networks, there exists
L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i}
with
L_{i}=\Theta(\log^{4}n)
,
\|W_{i}\|_{\infty}=\widetilde{\Theta}\big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{J}_{k})
,
R_{i}=\widetilde{\Theta}\big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{J}_{k})
, and
B_{i}=\exp(\Theta(\log^{4}n))
so that
\phi^{*}\in\Phi(L_{i},W_{i},R_{i},B_{i},c_{2}\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}})
. So we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\cdot\sup_{t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]}\sup_{x\,:\,\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}}\left\|\phi^{*}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n}R_{i}L_{i}\log\left(nL_{i}\left\|W_{i}\right\|_{\infty}B_{i}\right)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}t_{i}\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{4}}+t_{i}\frac{n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{2}}\Big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\Big).

Then note that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\leq\min_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sqrt{\log n}}
, we have

\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n}\gtrsim\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n}\gtrsim\frac{(\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n}\gtrsim\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{k}}{2}},

and if
d_{k}\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-d_{k}}}{n}\gtrsim\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-d_{k}+2}}{n(\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i})}\gtrsim\frac{\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}+2}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{k}-2}{2}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}\gtrsim\frac{\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{k}-2}{2}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}},

if
d_{k}=1
,

\displaystyle\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-2}}{n}\gtrsim\frac{1}{n(\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i})}\gtrsim\frac{\max_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+1}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}},

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\min(\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n})\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n})\Big),

and using
\beta_{k}\geq\alpha_{k}+1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[((t_{i}\log n)\wedge 1)\cdot\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\min(n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}+t_{i}\cdot n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},t_{i}\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n})\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{1}{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}+2}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{\sigma_{*}^{2-d_{k}}}{n})\Big).

B.3
Summarizing the results

Finally, by combining the results in case 1 and 2, we have for any
i\in[L]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}_{2}}\min(\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n})\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(\frac{n^{-\frac{2\beta_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}}{\sigma_{*}^{2}+t_{i}}+n^{-\frac{2\alpha_{k}}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{(\sigma_{*}+\sqrt{1\wedge t_{i}})^{-(d_{k}\vee 2)}}{n})\Big),

and using Lemma
23
, if
\sigma_{*}>n^{-\min_{k}\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\sqrt{\log n}
, we have
\tau\leq\frac{1}{n}\log n
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{1}\left(\widehat{p},P_{*}\right)]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sum_{i=0}^{L-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\big((t_{i}\log n)\wedge 1\big)\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t\right]}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{\sigma_{*}^{1-\frac{d_{k}}{2}}}{\sqrt{n}})\Big).

if
\sigma_{*}\leq n^{-\min_{k}\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\sqrt{\log n}
, we have
\tau\asymp n^{-\min_{k}\frac{2\alpha_{k}+2}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\cdot\log n
and

\frac{\sigma_{*}^{1-\frac{d_{k}}{2}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+n^{-\min_{k}\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{1}\left(\widehat{p},P_{*}\right)]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sum_{i=0}^{L-1}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\big((t_{i}\log n)\wedge 1\big)\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\widehat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t\right]}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min(n^{-\frac{\alpha_{k}+1}{2\alpha_{k}+d_{k}}},\frac{\sigma_{*}^{1-\frac{d_{k}}{2}}}{\sqrt{n}})\Big).

B.4
Proof for Lemma
24

Since
\mathcal{M}\subset\mathbb{B}_{1}(0_{D})
, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\nabla p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\Big]}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\Big]}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|x\|+\sqrt{D}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}.

Therefore, for any constant
c_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\frac{\|x\|+\sqrt{D}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n},\|x\|\geq c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)+1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n},\|x\|<c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\right)\,{\rm d}x\Big].

Note that for large enough
c_{1}
, using
\mathcal{M}\subset\mathbb{B}_{1}(0_{D})
and
\sigma_{\tau}\leq\sigma_{t_{i}}\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}\leq\sqrt{\sigma_{*}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2}}\leq\sqrt{2}
, we have for any
t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\frac{\|x\|+\sqrt{D}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n},\|x\|>c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\frac{\|x\|+\sqrt{D}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot 1\left(\|x\|>c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

Moreover, for large enough
c_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\frac{\|x\|+\sqrt{D}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n},\|x\|\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}+D}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}}\cdot\exp\big(-\frac{c_{0}^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}^{2}}{4\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\log n)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}1\left(\|x\|\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

Therefore, for sufficiently large
c_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\int\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x\leq c_{1}\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

Similarly, we can show

\displaystyle\int\left\|S(x,t)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int c^{2}\frac{\log n}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}p_{t}(x)\cdot 1\left(\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\geq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\,{\rm d}x\leq c^{2}c_{1}\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

The first statement is then proved. For the second statement, denote
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{D_{x}}\to[K]
so that
\kappa(x)\in\arg\min_{k\in[K]}{\rm dist}(x,\mathcal{M}_{k})
, and denote

{\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}(x)
as any point inside
{\arg\min}_{y\in\mathcal{M}_{k}}\|x-y\|
. Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
, we have

\displaystyle(2\pi\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})^{\frac{D}{2}}p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\omega_{\kappa(x)}\cdot\int_{y\in\mathbb{B}_{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}}({\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{M}_{\kappa(x)}}(x))\cap\mathcal{M}_{\kappa(x)}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\bigg)q_{\kappa(x)}(y)\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\geq C\exp(-\frac{(c_{0}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}+(1-m_{t}))^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}})\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{d}

\displaystyle\geq n^{-c_{2}}.

Therefore, there exists a constant
c_{0}^{\prime}
so that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
with
\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{M})\leq c_{0}\sigma_{t_{i}}\sqrt{\log n}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\Big(-\frac{x-m_{t}y}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\|x-m_{t}y\|\leq c_{0}^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\Big]}{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{y\sim Q_{k}}\Big[\exp\big(-\frac{\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}}\big)\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\|x-m_{t}y\|\leq c_{0}^{\prime}\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\Big]}\right\|+\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t}}\asymp\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{t_{i}}}.

We can then get the desired statement by combining all pieces.

Appendix C
Proofs for Section 5

C.1
Proof of Theorem
13

Let
m_{t}=\exp(-\int_{0}^{t}\beta_{s}ds)
and
\sigma_{t}^{2}=1-m_{t}^{2}
and note that
\sigma_{t}\to 0
as
t\to 0^{+}
. The marginal density at a particular time
t>0
can be written as

p_{t}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{N}(x;m_{t}x_{0},\sigma_{t}^{2}I)dP_{*}(x_{0})=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0}).

Hence, by differentiating under the integral,

\nabla p_{t}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{S}}-\frac{x-m_{t}x_{0}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})

Thus

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}=\frac{\int-(\frac{x-m_{t}x_{0}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})}{\int\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big(x-m_{t}\frac{\int x_{0}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})}{\int\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})}\Big)

First consider the case where
\mathcal{S}=M_{\ell}
. Let
n_{t}(x)
be the expression in parentheses above. By assumption
x
is sufficiently close to
\mathcal{S}
, so
x
is in a tubular region of
M_{\ell}
where
\pi_{\ell}
is unique. Recall Laplace’s method, which states that if
f
and
g
are smooth functions and
f
achieves a unique minimum at a point
y_{*}
in the interior of a region
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then

\int_{U}g(y)\exp(-Mf(y))\,dx\approx\Big(\frac{2\pi}{M}\Big)^{d/2}\frac{g(y_{*})\exp(-Mf(y_{*}))}{|\text{Hess}_{f}(y_{*})|^{1/2}}\quad\text{as}\quad M\to\infty.

One extends this to manifolds by using the intrinsic Hessian. We can hence express the integrals in our setting as
t\to 0
by

\int_{M_{\ell}}g(y)q_{\ell}(y)(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-D/2}\exp(-f_{t}(y)/\sigma_{t}^{2})d\text{vol}_{M_{\ell}}(y)\approx(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-(D-d_{\ell})/2}\frac{g(y_{*,t})q_{\ell}(y_{*,t})\exp(-f_{t}(y_{*,t})/\sigma_{t}^{2})}{\sqrt{\det(\text{Hess}_{M_{\ell}}f_{t}(y_{*,t}))}},

where
q_{\ell}
is the density of
Q_{\ell}
with respect to the volume measure on
M_{\ell}
,
f_{t}(y)=\frac{1}{2}\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}
, and at the minimizer
y_{*,t}
, the intrinsic Hessian is positive definite on
T_{y_{*,t}}M
.

For simplicity in the general stratified case, we combine the geometric and local density terms in the expression above into the function

G_{\ell}(x,t)=(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-(D-d_{\ell})/2}\frac{q_{\ell}(y_{*,t})\exp(-f_{t}(y_{*,t})/\sigma_{t}^{2})}{\sqrt{\det(\text{Hess}_{M_{\ell}}f_{t}(y_{*,t}))}}

Note that if
y_{*}=\arg\min_{y}\|x-y\|^{2}
, then

y_{*,t}=\arg\min_{y}\|x-m_{t}y\|^{2}=\arg\min_{y}\left\|x/m_{t}-y\right\|^{2}=\pi_{\ell}(x/m_{t}).

For all sufficiently small
t
,
x/m_{t}
lies in a tubular neighborhood of
M_{\ell}
where
\pi_{\ell}
is uniquely defined. Hence, there is a unique minimizer of
m_{t}M_{\ell}
given by
m_{t}y_{*,t}=\pi_{\ell,t}(x):=m_{t}\pi_{\ell}(x/m_{t})
. The integrals in the expression for
n_{t}(x)
only differ in the amplitude
g
, so, after applying Laplace’s method to the numerator and denominator and canceling terms,

n_{t}(x)\approx x-m_{t}y_{*,t}=x-\pi_{\ell,t}(x)\quad\text{ as }t\to 0^{+}.

Since
m_{t}\to 1
as
t\to 0^{+}
and the projection map is continuous in a tubular neighborhood of
M_{\ell}
, we have that
m_{t}y_{*,t}\to y_{*}
. Thus, as
t\to 0^{+}
,

\frac{\nabla\log p_{t}(x)}{\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|}=\frac{-n_{t}(x)}{\|n_{t}(x)\|}\to\frac{\pi_{\ell}(x)-x}{\|\pi_{\ell}(x)-x\|}.

General case.

Now recall that for a stratified space we obtain a mixture of the form

p_{t}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{S}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dP_{*}(x_{0})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\int_{M_{k}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0}),

and

\nabla p_{t}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\int_{M_{k}}-\frac{x-m_{t}x_{0}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0}).

The score is then realized as

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\int_{M_{k}}-(\frac{x-m_{t}x_{0}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0})}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\omega_{\ell}\int_{M_{\ell}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{\ell}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\left(x-m_{t}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\omega_{k}\frac{\int_{M_{k}}x_{0}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0})}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\omega_{\ell}\int_{M_{\ell}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{\ell}(x_{0})}\right)

Note that we need only consider the indices in the set

L(x)=\{k\in\{1,...,K\}:\operatorname{dist}(x,M_{k})=\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})\}.

Indeed, if
k\not\in L(x)
, then
\Delta=\text{dist}(x,M_{k})^{2}-\text{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})^{2}>0
. By compactness of
M_{k}
and
\mathcal{S}
and the fact that
m_{t}\to 1
as
t\to 0
, for sufficiently small
t>0
we have

\text{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{k})^{2}\geq\text{dist}(x,m_{t}\mathcal{S})^{2}+\frac{\Delta}{2}.

Define

I_{k}(t)=\int_{M_{k}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0}),\quad J_{k}(t)=\int_{M_{k}}x_{0}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}x_{0})dQ_{k}(x_{0}).

Then,

I_{k}(t)\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-D}\exp\left(-\frac{\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{k})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\leq\sigma_{t}^{-D}\exp\left(-\frac{\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}\mathcal{S})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\Delta}{4\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

On the other hand, for
\ell\in L(x)
, we have by Laplace’s method that, for small
t
,

I_{\ell}(t)\gtrsim\sigma_{t}^{-(D-d_{\ell})}\exp\left(-\frac{\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}\mathcal{S})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

Hence

\frac{I_{k}}{I_{\ell}}\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-d_{\ell}}\exp\left(-\frac{\Delta}{4\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\to 0.

This holds similarly for
J_{k}
in place of
I_{k}
above since
\|x_{0}\|
is bounded uniformly by compactness, so it only contributes to the constant.

Applying Laplace’s method to every integral for indices in
L(x)
, we have that

\displaystyle n_{t}(x)

\displaystyle\approx x-m_{t}\sum_{k\in L(x)}\omega_{k}\frac{G_{k}(x,t)y_{k,*,t}}{\sum_{\ell\in L(x)}\omega_{\ell}G_{\ell}(x,t)}

\displaystyle=x-\sum_{k\in L(x)}\gamma_{k,t}\cdot m_{t}y_{k,*,t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in L(x)}\gamma_{k,t}(x-m_{t}y_{k,*,t}),

where

\gamma_{k,t}=\frac{\omega_{k}G_{k}(x,t)}{\sum_{\ell\in L(x)}\omega_{\ell}G_{\ell}(x,t)}.

Then observe that as
t\to 0
,

\frac{G_{k}(x,t)}{G_{\ell}(x,t)}\asymp\sigma_{t}^{d_{k}-d_{\ell}}\exp\left(-\frac{\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{k})^{2}-\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{\ell})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

Note that for
k,\ell\in L(x)
, we have that
\operatorname{dist}(x,M_{k})=\operatorname{dist}(x,M_{\ell})=\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})
. Hence, by a first order expansion,

\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{k})^{2}=\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})^{2}+O(1-m_{t}),

\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{\ell})^{2}=\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathcal{S})^{2}+O(1-m_{t});

and so

\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{k})^{2}-\operatorname{dist}(x,m_{t}M_{\ell})^{2}=O(1-m_{t})=O(\sigma_{t}^{2}).

This implies that the exponential term in the quotient above is bounded, so that
G_{k}/G_{\ell}\to 0
as
\sigma_{t}^{d_{k}-d_{\ell}}\to 0
, which is the case if
d_{k}>d_{\ell}
. Hence as
t\to 0
, assuming that stratum
k_{*}
has unique minimal dimension,

\gamma_{k_{*},t}(x)\to 1,\quad\gamma_{k,t}(x)\to 0\quad\text{for $k\neq k_{*}$}.

Similar to the manifold case, letting
t\to 0
implies that

\frac{\nabla\log p_{t}(x)}{\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|}\to\frac{\pi_{k_{*}}(x)-x}{\|\pi_{k_{*}}(x)-x\|}.

\hfill{\Box}

C.2
Proof of Lemma
15

Let
Y
be a
d
-dimensional manifold and
x_{0}\in Y
. Diffused points are of the form
x_{t}=m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}z
,
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{D})
.
35
expand the score function of a VE-SDE into a normal part with bounded remainder for points off the manifold within a small tubular neighborhood. In our VP setting,

p_{t}(x)=\int_{Y}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-m_{t}y)p_{*}(y)d\operatorname{vol}_{Y}(y)=\int_{m_{t}Y}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}(x-z)m_{t}^{-d}p_{*}(z/m_{t})d\operatorname{vol}_{m_{t}Y}(z),

where the last equality follows from reparameterization. Hence,
p_{t}
is a Gaussian smoothing of the scaled manifold
m_{t}Y
and the VP-SDE analogue of Theorem 3.1 in
35
is:

\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=-\frac{x-\pi_{t}(x)}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}+O(1),

where
\pi_{t}(x)=m_{t}\pi(x/m_{t})
is the projection onto
m_{t}Y
and
x\notin m_{t}Y
is in a small tubular neighborhood of
m_{t}Y
. For
x_{t}=m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}z
in a tubular neighborhood of
m_{t}Y
, we have that

x_{t}-\pi_{t}(x_{t})=(m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}z)-m_{t}\pi(x_{0}+(\sigma_{t}/m_{t})z).

By applying a Taylor expansion to
\pi
, we have that

\pi(x_{0}+(\sigma_{t}/m_{t})z)=x_{0}+(\sigma_{t}/m_{t})P_{T}z+O(\sigma_{t}^{2}\|z\|^{2}),

where
P_{T}
is projection onto the tangent space of of
Y
at
x_{0}
. Substitution yields

x_{t}-\pi_{t}(x_{t})=\sigma_{t}z-\sigma_{t}P_{T}z+O(\sigma_{t}^{2}\|z\|^{2})=\sigma_{t}P_{N}z+O(\sigma_{t}^{2}\|z\|^{2}),

where
P_{N}=I-P_{T}
is the projection onto the normal space of
Y
at
x_{0}
.
Hence, we deduce that

\nabla\log p_{t}(x_{t})=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}P_{N}z+R_{t},\quad\text{where}\quad\sup_{t\in[t_{\operatorname{start}},t_{\operatorname{end}}]}\mathbb{E}[\|R_{t}\|^{2}\mid t]\leq C

for
x_{t}\not\in m_{t}Y
in a sufficiently small tubular neighborhood of
m_{t}Y
. Then

\mathbb{E}[\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\nabla\log p_{t}(x)^{\top}\mid t]=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}P_{N}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\mathbb{E}[P_{N}zR_{t}^{\top}+R_{t}z^{\top}P_{N}\mid t]+\mathbb{E}[R_{t}R_{t}^{\top}\mid t].

By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\|\mathbb{E}[P_{N}zR_{t}^{\top}\mid t]\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|P_{N}z\|^{2}]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\|R_{t}\|^{2}\mid t]^{1/2}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|P_{N}z\|^{2}]^{1/2}\sqrt{C}\leq\sqrt{D-d}\sqrt{C}=O(1),

where we used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\|P_{N}z\|^{2}]=\operatorname{trace}(P_{N})=D-d
. We also have that

\|\mathbb{E}[R_{t}R_{t}^{\top}\mid t]\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|R_{t}\|^{2}|t]\leq C.

Thus,

\mathbb{E}[\nabla\log p_{t}(X)\nabla\log p_{t}(X)^{\top}|t]=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}P_{N}+O(\sigma_{t}^{-1}).

Now if we average over
t\in[t_{\text{start}},t_{\text{end}}]
, we have that

\Sigma(x_{0})=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right]P_{N}+O(\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{t}^{-1}]).

Since
Y
is
d
-dimensional, the eigenvalues of
P_{N}
consist of
D-d
ones and
d
zeros. Arranging them in descending order implies that the eigenvalues
\mu_{k}
of
\Sigma(x_{0})
satisfy:

\mu_{k}=\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{t}^{2}]+O(\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{t}^{-1}]),\quad k\leq D-d;

\mu_{k}=O(\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{t}^{-1}]),\quad k>D-d.

Let
g=\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{t}^{2}]
and
h=\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{t}^{-1}]
. Then we can write the eigenvalues as

\mu_{k}=\begin{cases}g+O(h),&k\leq D-d;\\
O(h),&k>D-d.\end{cases}

For
k<D-d
,

\mu_{k}-\mu_{k+1}=(g+O(h))-(g+O(h))=O(h).

For
k>D-d
, we have

\mu_{k}-\mu_{k+1}=O(h)

Lastly, for
k=D-d
,

\mu_{D-d}-\mu_{D-d+1}=(g+O(h))-O(h)=\Theta(g)

because
h=o(g)
by Jensen’s inequality. Since
g=\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{t}^{2}]\geq 1/\sigma_{t_{\text{end}}}^{2}\to\infty
as
t_{\text{end}}\to 0
, the gap between indices
D-d
and
D-d+1
will dominate all other gaps for sufficiently small
t_{\text{end}}
.

\hfill{\Box}

C.3
Proof of Theorem
16

Let
x_{0}\in M_{\ell}\subseteq S\setminus S_{\text{sing}}
,
\delta=d(x_{0},S_{\text{sing}})>0
. Note that
t_{2}^{(n)}\to 0
implies that
\sigma_{t_{2}^{(n)}}\lesssim\delta/2
for sufficiently large
n
, making other distant strata have negligible effect on the score, allowing us to localize on
M_{\ell}
and apply Lemma
15
(see proof of Lemma
13
for justification). Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{1:n}\sim P_{*}^{\otimes n}}\bigg[\frac{1}{\Delta t_{n}}\int_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{t^{(n)}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\|\widehat{S}_{n}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x)\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\bigg]\leq\epsilon_{n},

where
\Delta t_{n}=t_{2}^{(n)}-t_{1}^{(n)}
. Note that
\widehat{S}_{n}
is trained on an independent sample
X_{1:n}\sim P_{*}^{\otimes n}
, while the test points
\{(x_{i},t_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N_{n}}
are drawn independently from the training data.

Let
R_{n}\geq 1
. For any
t>0
, consider the sets
B_{t}=\{x:||x-m_{t}x_{0}||\leq R_{n}\sigma_{t}\}
and
U_{t}=B(x_{0},\frac{1}{R_{n}}\sigma_{t})\cap M_{\ell}
. We get a lower bound on the marginal distribution
p_{t}
via concentration on
U_{t}
:

p_{t}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}p_{t}(x|x^{\prime})P_{*}(dx^{\prime})\geq\int_{U_{t}}p_{t}(x|x^{\prime})P_{*}(dx^{\prime})

Recall that
p_{t}(x|y)=\mathcal{N}(m_{t}y,\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{D})
. Hence, for
x\in B_{t}
and
x^{\prime}\in U_{t}
,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t}(x|x_{0})}{p_{t}(x|x^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\frac{\|x-m_{t}x^{\prime}\|^{2}-\|x-m_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\frac{m_{t}^{2}\|x^{\prime}-x_{0}\|^{2}-2m_{t}\langle x-m_{t}x_{0},x^{\prime}-x_{0}\rangle}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\frac{m_{t}^{2}R_{n}^{-2}+2m_{t}R_{n}R_{n}^{-1}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{R_{n}^{-2}+2}{2}\right)

This implies that

p_{t}(x|x_{0})\leq c^{\prime}p_{t}(x|x^{\prime})

for some constant
c^{\prime}\leq e^{3/2}
since
R_{n}\geq 1
. Then, since
P_{*}
is bounded from below on
M_{\ell}
, we have that
P_{*}(U_{t})\gtrsim R_{n}^{-d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t}^{d_{\ell}}
. Putting everything together and combining constants into
\tilde{C}
with the fact that
\sigma_{t}
is monotonic in
t
gives that over the interval
[t_{1}^{(n)},t_{2}^{(n)}]
,

p_{t}(x|x_{0})\leq\tilde{C}R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t}^{-d_{\ell}}p_{t}(x)\leq\tilde{C}R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t^{(n)}_{1}}^{-d_{\ell}}p_{t}(x)

On
B_{t}
, we can use the upper bound on score approximation to get

\mathbb{E}_{X_{1:n}\sim P_{*}^{\otimes n}}\Big[\frac{1}{\Delta t_{n}}\int_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{t_{2}^{(n)}}\int_{B_{t}}||\hat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)||^{2}p_{t}(x|x_{0})dxdt\Big]\leq\tilde{C}R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-d_{\ell}}\epsilon_{n}.

For a bound on
B_{t}^{c}
we use the following lemma.

Lemma 31
.

Suppose
\|\widehat{S}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq V_{n}
and
\operatorname{supp}P_{*}\subseteq B(0,L)
. Then for
X\sim p_{t}(\cdot|x_{0})
,

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}_{n}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}]\lesssim\Big(V_{n}^{2}+\frac{L^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}+\frac{R^{2}+D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big)e^{-(R-\sqrt{D})^{2}/2}

Now let
T_{n}(R_{n})
denote the expectation bound from the lemma with
R=R_{n}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\Delta t_{n}}\int_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{t_{2}^{(n)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\|\hat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}p_{t}(x|x_{0})dxdt\right]\leq\tilde{C}R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-d_{\ell}}\epsilon_{n}+T_{n}(R_{n}).

Let
E_{n}
be the term inside the expectation and let
\rho_{n}=\tilde{C}R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-d_{\ell}}\epsilon_{n}+T_{n}(R_{n})
. Choose
R_{n}\to\infty
such that
T_{n}(R_{n})\leq\eta_{n}\to 0
for some decreasing sequence
\eta_{n}
. Note that this also affects the size of
B_{t}
, but one can take
R_{n}
to grow slowly, such as
R_{n}=O(\sqrt{\log n})
assuming mild conditions such as polynomial growth of
V_{n}
,
\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1}
and
1/\eta_{n}
. This means that
R_{n}^{d_{\ell}}=O((\log n)^{d_{\ell}/2})=o(n^{\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
. We then have that
T_{n}(R_{n})\lesssim\eta_{n}\to 0
, and by our assumption that
n^{\eta}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-d_{\ell}}\epsilon_{n}\to 0
, we have
\rho_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
by choosing any
\gamma<\eta
.

The term
E_{n}
depends on
X_{1:n}
, so we define the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}=\{E_{n}(X_{1:n})\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\}
. Then by Markov’s inequality,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c})\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Let
\Sigma_{n}=\Sigma(x_{0})
as in Lemma
15
, i.e.

\Sigma_{n}=\mathbb{E}_{t\sim\operatorname{Uniform}[t_{1}^{(n)},t_{2}^{(n)}],X\sim p_{t}(\cdot|x_{0})}[\nabla\log p_{t}(X)\nabla\log p_{t}(X)^{\top}]

and define

\hat{\Sigma}_{n}=\frac{1}{N_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\hat{S}_{n}(x_{i},t_{i})\hat{S}_{n}(x_{i},t_{i})^{\top},

Letting
s_{i}=\nabla\log p_{t_{i}}(x_{i})
and
\hat{S}_{i}=\hat{S}_{n}(x_{i},t_{i})
, we have

\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}=\underbrace{\frac{1}{N_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}\left[\hat{S}_{n}(x_{i},t_{i})\hat{S}_{n}(x_{i},t_{i})^{\top}-s_{i}s_{i}^{\top}\right]}_{(A)}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{N_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n}}s_{i}s_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma_{n}}_{(B)}.

Bounding
(A)
:
Using the relation
\hat{S}_{i}\hat{S}_{i}^{\top}-s_{i}s_{i}^{\top}=(\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i})(\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i})^{\top}+(\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i})s_{i}^{\top}+s_{i}(\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i})^{\top}
implies that

\|\hat{S}_{i}\hat{S}_{i}^{\top}-s_{i}s_{i}^{\top}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|^{2}+2\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|\|s_{i}\|.

Taking expectation with respect to
t\sim\operatorname{Uniform}[t_{1}^{(n)},t_{2}^{(n)}]
and
x_{i}\sim p_{t}(\cdot|x_{0})
and applying Cauchy-Schwarz,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(A)\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}|X_{1:n}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|^{2}|X_{1:n}]+2\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|^{2}|X_{1:n}]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}.

We have that
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|^{2}\mid X_{1:n}]\leq\rho_{n}^{1/2}
on
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. For the second term, note that, by Jensen’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{4}]^{1/2}.

Lemma 32
.

Let
x_{0}\in M_{\ell}
satisfy
\operatorname{dist}(x_{0},\mathcal{S}_{\operatorname{sing}})>\delta
. Then for sufficiently large
n
,

\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p_{T}(X)\|^{4}]\lesssim\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-4},

where
T\sim\operatorname{Uniform}[t_{1}^{(n)},t_{2}^{(n)}]
and
X|T=t\sim p_{t}(\cdot\mid x_{0})
.

By Lemma
32
, we hence have that
\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}\lesssim\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1}
. Thus

2\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}_{i}-s_{i}\|^{2}\mid X_{1:n}]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{2}]^{1/2}\leq 2\left(\rho_{n}^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot O(\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1})=O\left(\rho_{n}^{1/4}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1}\right).

Let
\zeta_{n}
be the upper bound on the total expectation above and note that since
\rho_{n}\to 0
, we have that
\zeta_{n}=O(\rho_{n}^{1/2}+\rho_{n}^{1/4}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1})=o(\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-2})
. For any fixed
c>0
, we have by Markov that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|(A)\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}>c\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-2}\mid X_{1:n}\right)\leq\frac{\zeta_{n}}{c\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-2}}\to 0.

Bounding
(B)
:

Note that
\|(B)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\|(B)\|_{F}
and denote
Y_{n,i}=s_{i}s_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma_{n}
. Then, conditioned on the sample
X_{1:n}
used to train
\hat{S}_{n}
,
Y_{n,i}
are i.i.d., and hence

\mathbb{E}[\|(B)\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}\mid X_{1:n}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|(B)\|_{F}^{2}\mid X_{1:n}]=\frac{1}{N_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|Y_{n,1}\|_{F}^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{N_{n}}\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{4}].

From Lemma
32
, we have that

\mathbb{E}[\|s_{i}\|^{4}]\lesssim\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-4}
, so

\mathbb{E}[\|(B)\|^{2}_{\operatorname{op}}\mid X_{1:n}]\lesssim\frac{1}{N_{n}}\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-4}.

Hence, by Chebyshev,

\mathbb{P}(\|(B)\|_{\operatorname{op}}>C\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-2})\lesssim\frac{1}{C^{2}N_{n}}\to 0

as
N_{n}\to\infty
.

Let
\hat{\mu}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\hat{\mu}_{D}
be the eigenvalues of
\hat{\Sigma}_{n}
and
\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{D}
be the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{n}
. By Weyl’s inequality,

|\hat{\mu}_{k}-\mu_{k}|\leq\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}}.

By Lemma
15
, for sufficiently small
t_{2}^{(n)}
, the largest spectral gap
\gamma_{n}\asymp\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-2}
of
\Sigma_{n}
occurs between indices
D-d_{\ell}
and
D-d_{\ell}+1
, and the rest of the consecutive spectral gaps are of order
\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{t}^{-1}]
.

On the event
\{\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}}\leq\gamma_{n}/8\}
, we thus have for sufficiently large
n
,

\hat{\mu}_{k}-\hat{\mu}_{k+1}\geq\mu_{k}-\mu_{k+1}-2\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}},

so

\hat{\mu}_{D-d_{\ell}}-\hat{\mu}_{D-d_{\ell}+1}\geq\gamma_{n}-2\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}}>3\gamma_{n}/4.

For any other
k\neq D-d_{\ell}
,

\hat{\mu}_{k}-\hat{\mu}_{k+1}\leq\mu_{k}-\mu_{k+1}+2\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}}<\sigma_{t_{1}^{(n)}}^{-1}+2\|\hat{\Sigma}_{n}-\Sigma_{n}\|_{\operatorname{op}}<\gamma_{n}/2

for sufficiently large
n
as
\gamma_{n}\asymp\sigma_{t}^{-2}\to\infty
, which means that
\hat{d}_{n}(x_{0})=d_{\ell}
.
Now, we have that

\mathbb{P}(\hat{d}_{n}(x_{0})\neq d_{\ell})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(\{\|(A)\|_{\operatorname{op}}>\gamma_{n}/8\}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n})+\mathbb{P}(\|(B)\|_{\operatorname{op}}>\gamma_{n}/8)\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

\hfill{\Box}

C.4
Proof of Lemma
31

Proof:
We first note that

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}]\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}]+2\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X)\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}],

where
X\sim\mathcal{N}(m_{t}x_{0},\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{D})
. Now note that
x=m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}z
for some
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{D})
, and, by definition of our network class,
\|\hat{S}\|_{\infty}\leq V_{n}
. For
R\gtrsim\sqrt{D}
, first term on the right side of the inequality can thus be bounded as:

2\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{S}(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}]\lesssim 2V_{n}^{2}e^{-(R-\sqrt{D})^{2}/2}.

As for the second term, we have by Tweedie’s formula that

\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big(x-m_{t}\mathbb{E}[x_{0}|X_{t}=x]\Big).

Thus

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}(\|x\|+m_{t}\|\mathbb{E}[x_{0}|X_{t}=x]\|).

Since the support of the true distribution
P_{*}
is compact, we have that
\|\mathbb{E}[x_{0}|X_{t}=x]\|<L
for some constant
L>0
. We also have that

\|x\|=\|m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}z\|\leq\|m_{t}x_{0}\|+\sigma_{t}\|z\|,

which implies that on
B_{t}^{c}
(where
\|z\|>R
):

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\|z\|>R\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}\Big(2\|x\|^{2}+2m_{t}^{2}\|\mathbb{E}[x_{0}|X_{t}=x]\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}(4\|m_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+4\sigma_{t}^{2}\|z\|^{2}+2m_{t}^{2}L^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}(3m_{t}^{2}L^{2}+2\sigma_{t}^{2}\|z\|^{2})

where the last inequality holds since
x_{0}\in\operatorname{supp}P_{*}
. Now by taking expectation, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t}^{c}}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}\mathbb{P}(\|z\|>R)+\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|z\|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\{\|z\|>R\}}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{L^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}e^{-(R-\sqrt{D})^{2}/2}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}(R^{2}+D)e^{-(R-\sqrt{D})^{2}/2}

Combining the two bounds together gives us the lemma.
\hfill{\Box}

C.5
Proof of Lemma
32

Recall that a diffusion point from
x_{0}
takes the form
X_{t}=m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z
, where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{D})
. From the proof of Lemma
15
, we see that for a small tubular region around
M_{\ell}
,

\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})=-\frac{X_{t}-\pi_{t}(X_{t})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}+R_{t}(X_{t}),

where
\|R_{t}(X_{t})\|\leq C
uniformly in
t\in[t_{1}^{(n)},t_{2}^{(n)}]
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{t}
be the event that this decomposition holds. Then
\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}\subseteq\{\|Z\|>r/\sigma_{t}\}
for some radius
r>0
. On the event
\mathcal{A}_{t}
, since
\pi_{t}(x)
is the minimizer of the distance to
m_{t}M_{\ell}
, we have that

\|X_{t}-\pi_{t}(X_{t})\|\leq\|X_{t}-m_{t}x_{0}\|\leq\sigma_{t}\|Z\|,

and so

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|\leq\frac{\|Z\|}{\sigma_{t}}+\|R_{t}(X_{t})\|.

From the inequality
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8(a^{4}+b^{4})
, we have that

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{4}\lesssim\frac{\|Z\|^{4}}{\sigma_{t}^{4}}+1

Then

\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\mid t]\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-4}\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{4}]+1\lesssim\sigma_{t^{(n)}_{1}}^{-4},

where we used the fact that
\sigma_{t}
is monotonically increasing and
\|Z\|^{2}\sim\chi_{D}^{2}
, and we have for a
\chi_{D}^{2}
random variable that
\mathbb{E}[(\|Z\|^{2})^{2}]=D^{2}+2D
.
On the event
\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}
, we note that the manifold
M_{\ell}
is compact, so that
\|M_{\ell}\|\leq L<\infty
, and

\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=\frac{m_{t}\mathbb{E}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]-x}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Thus,

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{m_{t}L+\|x\|}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Plugging in
X_{t}=m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z
implies that

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|\leq\frac{L}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\|m_{t}x_{0}+\sigma_{t}Z\|}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\|Z\|}{\sigma_{t}},

and so

\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{4}\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-8}+\sigma_{t}^{-4}\|Z\|^{4}.

Now, by Gaussian tails, we have that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}\mid t)\lesssim e^{-a/\sigma_{t}^{2}}
for some constant
a>0
, and
\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}\mid t]
similarly decays like
e^{-a/\sigma_{t}^{2}}
up to a polynomial factor. Hence

\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}\mid t]\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-8}e^{-a/\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\sigma_{t}^{-4}\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}}]=o(\sigma_{t}^{-4}).

Putting everything together gives us the claim.
\hfill{\Box}
